# An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural Networks^(†)^(†)thanks: Submitted on 28 December 2023. \fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).

Yue Wang Department of Applied Mathematics, Hong Kong Polytechnic University, Hong Kong, China (). <yueyue.wang@connect.polyu.hk>    Chao Zhang School of Mathematics and Statistics, Beijing Jiaotong University, Beijing 100044, China (). <zc.njtu@163.com>    Xiaojun Chen Department of Applied Mathematics, Hong Kong Polytechnic University, Hong Kong, China (). <maxjchen@polyu.edu.hk>

###### Abstract

Recurrent Neural Networks (RNNs) are widely used to model sequential data in a wide range of areas, such as natural language processing, speech recognition, machine translation, and time series analysis. In this paper, we model the training process of RNNs with the ReLU activation function as a constrained optimization problem with a smooth nonconvex objective function and piecewise smooth nonconvex constraints. We prove that any feasible point of the optimization problem satisfies the no nonzero abnormal multiplier constraint qualification (NNAMCQ), and any local minimizer is a Karush-Kuhn-Tucker (KKT) point of the problem. Moreover, we propose an augmented Lagrangian method (ALM) and design an efficient block coordinate descent (BCD) method to solve the subproblems of the ALM. The update of each block of the BCD method has a closed-form solution. The stop criterion for the inner loop is easy to check and can be stopped in finite steps. Moreover, we show that the BCD method can generate a directional stationary point of the subproblem. Furthermore, we establish the global convergence of the ALM to a KKT point of the constrained optimization problem. Compared with the state-of-the-art algorithms, numerical results demonstrate the efficiency and effectiveness of the ALM for training RNNs.

###### keywords:

recurrent neural network, nonsmooth nonconvex optimization, augmented Lagrangian method, block coordinate descent

{MSCcodes}

65K05, 90B10, 90C26, 90C30

## 1 Introduction

Recurrent Neural Networks (RNNs) have been applied in a wide range of areas, such as speech recognition \[[15](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib15), [27](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib27)\], natural language processing \[[22](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib22), [28](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib28)\] and nonlinear time series forecasting \[[1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib1), [23](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib23)\]. In this paper, we focus on the Elman RNN architecture \[[13](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib13)\], one of the earliest and most fundamental RNNs, and use Elman RNNs to deal with the regression task with the least squares loss function.

Given input data $`x_{t} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{n}`$ and output data $`y_{t} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{m}`$, $`t = {1,\ldots,T}`$, a widely used minimization problem for training RNNs is represented as (see \[[14](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib14), pp. 381\])

```math
{{\min\limits_{A,W,V,b,c}\frac{1}{T}}{\sum\limits_{t = 1}^{T}\left\| {y_{t} - \left( {{A\sigma\left( {{W\left( {\ldots\sigma{({{Vx_{1}} + b})}\ldots} \right)} + {Vx_{t}} + b} \right)} + c} \right)} \right\|^{2}}},
```

where $`W \in {\mathbb{R}}^{r \times r}`$, $`V \in {\mathbb{R}}^{r \times n}`$ and $`A \in {\mathbb{R}}^{m \times r}`$ are unknown weight matrices, $`b \in {\mathbb{R}}^{r}`$ and $`c \in {\mathbb{R}}^{m}`$ are unknown bias vectors, and $`\sigma:{{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ is a nonsmooth activation function that is applied component-wise on vectors and transforms the previous information and the input data $`x_{t}`$ into the hidden layer at time $`t`$. The training process by [Eq. 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S1.E1 "In 1 Introduction ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") can be interpreted as looking for proper weight matrices $`A,`$ $`W,`$ $`V,`$ and bias vectors $`b,`$ $`c`$ in RNNs to minimize the difference between the true value $`y_{t}`$ and the output from RNNs across all time steps. It is worth mentioning that the Elman RNNs in ([1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S1.E1 "Equation 1 ‣ 1 Introduction ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) shares the same weight matrices and bias vectors at different time steps \[[14](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib14), pp. 374\].

When the traditional backpropagation through time (BPTT) method is used to train RNNs, the highly nonlinear and nonsmooth composition function presented in [Eq. 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S1.E1 "In 1 Introduction ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") poses significant challenges. Gradient descent methods (GDs), as well as stochastic gradient descent-based methods (SGDs), are widely used to train RNNs in practice \[[8](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib8), [30](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib30)\], but the “gradient” of the loss function associated with the weighted matrices via the “chain rule” is calculated even if the “chain rule” does not hold. The “gradients” might exponentially increase to a very large value or shrink to zero as time $`t`$ increases, which makes RNNs training with large time length $`T`$ very challenging \[[4](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib4)\]. To overcome this shortcoming, various techniques have been developed, such as gradient clipping \[[22](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib22)\], gradient descent with Nesterov momentum \[[3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib3)\], initialization with small values \[[24](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib24)\], adding sparse regularization \[[2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib2)\], and so on. Because the essence of the above methods is to restrict the initial values of weighted matrices or gradients, they are sensitive to the choice of initial values \[[18](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib18)\]. Moreover, GDs and SGDs for training RNNs lack rigorous convergence analysis.

The objective function in ([1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S1.E1 "Equation 1 ‣ 1 Introduction ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) is nonsmooth nonconvex and has a highly composite structure. In this paper, we equivalently reformulate ([1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S1.E1 "Equation 1 ‣ 1 Introduction ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) as a constrained optimization problem with a simple smooth objective function by utilizing auxiliary variables to represent the composition structures and treating these representations as constraints. Moreover, we propose an augmented Lagrangian method (ALM) for the constrained optimization problem with $`\ell_{2}`$-norm regularization, and design a block coordinate descent (BCD) method to solve the subproblem of the ALM at every iteration. The solution of the subproblems of the BCD method is very easy to compute with a closed-form. Utilizing auxiliary variables to reformulate highly nonlinear composite structured problems as constrained optimization problems has been adopted for training Deep Neural Networks (DNNs) \[[7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib7), [12](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib12), [19](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib19), [20](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib20), [31](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib31)\]. However, these algorithms for DNNs cannot be used for RNNs directly because of the difference between their architectures. In fact, RNNs share the same weighted matrices and bias vectors across different layers, whereas DNNs have distinct weighted matrices and bias vectors in different layers. In DNNs, the weighted matrices and bias vectors can be updated layer by layer, allowing for the separation of the gradient calculation across different layers. However, in RNNs, the weighted matrices and bias vectors need to be updated simultaneously. Therefore, it is necessary to establish effective algorithms tailored to the characteristics of RNNs. To the best of our knowledge, the proposed ALM in this paper is the first first-order optimization method for training RNNs with solid convergence results.

Recently, several augmented Lagrangian-based methods have been proposed for nonconvex nonsmooth problems with composite structures. In \[[9](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib9)\], Chen et al. proposed an ALM for non-Lipschitz nonconvex programming, which requires the constraints to be smooth. Hallak and Teboulle in \[[16](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib16)\] transformed a comprehensive class of optimization problems into constrained problems with smooth constraints and nonsmooth nonconvex objective functions, and proposed a novel adaptive augmented Lagrangian-based method to solve the constrained problem. The assumption on the smoothness of constraints in \[[9](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib9), [16](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib16)\] is not satisfied for the optimization problem arising in training RNNs with nonsmooth activation functions considered in this paper.

Our contributions are summarized as follows:

- •
  We prove that the solution set of the constrained problem with $`\ell_{2}`$ regularization is nonempty and compact. Furthermore, we prove that any feasible point of the constrained optimization problem satisfies the no nonzero abnormal multiplier constraint qualification (NNAMCQ), which immediately guarantees any local minimizer of the constrained problems is a Karush-Kuhn-Tucker (KKT) point.
- •
  We show that any accumulation point of the sequence generated by the BCD method is a directional stationary point of the subproblem. Moreover, we show that in the $`k`$-th iteration of the ALM, the stopping criterion of the BCD method for solving the subproblem can be satisfied within $`O\left( {1/{(\epsilon_{k - 1})}^{2}} \right)`$ finite steps for any $`\epsilon_{k - 1} > 0`$.
- •
  We show that there exists an accumulation point of the sequence generated by the ALM for solving the constrained optimization problem with regularization and any accumulation point of the sequence is a KKT point.
- •
  We compare the performance of the ALM with several state-of-the-art methods for both synthetic and real datasets. The numerical results verify that our ALM outperforms other algorithms in terms of forecasting accuracy for both the training sets and the test sets.

The rest of the paper is organized as follows. In [Section 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2 "2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), we equivalently reformulate problem ([1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S1.E1 "Equation 1 ‣ 1 Introduction ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) as a nonsmooth nonconvex constrained minimization problem with a simple smooth objective function. Then we show that the solution set of the constrained problem with regularization is nonempty and bounded, and give the first-order necessary optimality conditions for the constrained problem and the regularized problem. We propose the ALM for the constrained problem with regularization, as well as the BCD method for the subproblems of the ALM in LABEL:sec:alg. We establish the convergence results of the BCD method, and the ALM in [Section 4](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4 "4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). Finally, we conduct numerical experiments on both the synthetic and real data in [Section 5](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S5 "5 Numerical experiments ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), which demonstrate the effectiveness and efficiency of the ALM for the reformulated optimization problem.

Notation and terminology. Let $`{\mathbb{N}}_{+}`$ denote the set of positive integers. For column vectors $`\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\ldots,\pi_{l}`$, let us denote by $`{\mathbf{π}}:={(\pi_{1};\pi_{2};\ldots;\pi_{l})} = {(\pi_{1}^{\top},\pi_{2}^{\top},\ldots,\pi_{l}^{\top})}^{\top}`$ a column vector. For a given matrix $`D \in {\mathbb{R}}^{k \times l}`$, we denote by $`D_{.j}`$ the $`j`$-th column of $`D`$ and use $`{{vec}{(D)}} = {(D_{.1};D_{.2};\ldots;D_{.l})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{kl}`$ to represent a column vector. For a given vector $`g`$, we use $`{diag}{(g)}`$ to represent the diagonal matrix, whose $`(i,i)`$-entry is the $`i`$-th component $`g_{i}`$ of $`g`$. We use $`{\mathbf{e}}_{l}`$ to represent the vector of all ones in $`{\mathbb{R}}^{l}`$. For $`\nu \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`\lceil\nu\rceil`$ refers to the smallest integer that is greater than $`\nu`$. For a given $`N \in {\mathbb{N}}_{+}`$, we denote $`{{\lbrack N\rbrack}:={\{ 1,2,\ldots,N\}}}.`$ We use $`\parallel \cdot \parallel`$ and $`\parallel \cdot \parallel_{\infty}`$ to denote the $`\ell_{2}`$-norm and infinity norm of a vector or a matrix, respectively. We denote by $`\parallel \cdot \parallel_{F}`$ the Frobenius norm of a matrix.

Let $`f:{{\mathbb{R}}^{n_{1}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a proper lower semicontinuous function defined on $`{\mathbb{R}}^{n_{1}}`$. The notation $`x^{k}\overset{f}{\rightarrow}\overline{x}`$ means that $`x^{k}\rightarrow\overline{x}`$ and $`{f{(x^{k})}}\rightarrow{f{(\overline{x})}}`$. The Fréchet subdifferential $`\hat{\partial}f{(x)}`$ and the limiting subdifferential $`\partial{f{(x)}}`$ of $`f`$ at $`\overline{x} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{n_{1}}`$ are defined as

$`{{\hat{\partial}f{(\overline{x})}}:=\left\{ {g \in {\mathbb{R}}^{n_{1}}}:{{\operatorname{lim\ inf}\limits_{{x\rightarrow\overline{x}},{x \neq \overline{x}}}\frac{{f{(x)}} - {f{(\overline{x})}} - {\langle g,{x - \overline{x}}\rangle}}{\|{x - \overline{x}}\|}} \geq 0} \right\}},`$$`{{\partial{f{(\overline{x})}}}:=\left\{ {g \in {\mathbb{R}}^{n_{1}}}:{{{{\exists x^{k}}\overset{f}{\rightarrow}\overline{x}},{g^{k}\rightarrow{g\text{with}g^{k}} \in {\hat{\partial}f{(x^{k})}}}},{\forall k}} \right\}},`$

by \[[17](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib17), Definition 1.1\] and \[[26](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib26), Definition 8.3, pp. 301\], respectively. A point $`\overline{x}`$ is said to be a Fréchet stationary point of $`{\min f}{(x)}`$ if $`0 \in {\hat{\partial}f{(\overline{x})}}`$, and $`\overline{x}`$ is said to be a limiting stationary point of $`{\min f}{(x)}`$ if $`0 \in {\partial{f{(\overline{x})}}}`$. By \[[11](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib11), pp. 30\], the usual (one-side) directional derivative of $`f`$ at $`x`$ in the direction $`d \in {\mathbb{R}}^{n_{1}}`$ is

```math
{{f'{(x;d)}}:={\lim\limits_{\lambda \downarrow 0}\frac{{f{({x + {\lambda d}})}} - {f{(x)}}}{\lambda}}},
```

when the limit exists. According to \[[25](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib25), Definition 2.1\], we say that a point $`\overline{x} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{n_{1}}`$ is a d(irectional)-stationary point of $`{\min f}{(x)}`$ if

```math
{{{f'{(\overline{x};d)}} \geq 0},{{\forall d} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{n_{1}}}}.
```

## 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions

For simplicity, we focus on the activation function $`\sigma:{{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ as the ReLU function, i.e.,

$`{{\sigma{(u)}} = {\max{\{ u,0\}}} = {(u)}_{+}}.`$

Our model, algorithms and theoretical analysis developed in this paper can be generalized to the leaky ReLU and the ELU activation functions. Detailed analysis for the extensions will be given in section [4.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.SS3 "4.3 Extensions to other activation functions ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

### 2.1 Problem reformulation

We utilize auxiliary variables $`\mathbf{h}`$, $`\mathbf{u}`$ and denote vectors $`\mathbf{w},\mathbf{a},\mathbf{z},\mathbf{s}`$ as

$`{{\mathbf{h} = {(h_{1};h_{2};\ldots;h_{T})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{rT}},{\mathbf{u} = {(u_{1};u_{2};\ldots;u_{T})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{rT}}},`$$`{{\mathbf{w} = {({\text{vec}{(W)}};{\text{vec}{(V)}};b)} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{N_{\mathbf{w}}}},{\mathbf{a} = {({\text{vec}{(A)}};c)} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{N_{\mathbf{a}}}}},`$$`{{\mathbf{z} = {(\mathbf{w};\mathbf{a})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{N_{\mathbf{w}} + N_{\mathbf{a}}}},{\mathbf{s} = {(\mathbf{z};\mathbf{h};\mathbf{u})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{N_{\mathbf{w}} + N_{\mathbf{a}} + {2rT}}}},`$

where $`N_{\mathbf{w}} = {r^{2} + {rn} + r}`$ and $`N_{\mathbf{a}} = {{mr} + m}`$.

We reformulate problem ([1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S1.E1 "Equation 1 ‣ 1 Introduction ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) as the following constrained optimization problem:

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
\min\limits_{\substack{\mathbf{s}}} & {\frac{1}{T}{\sum\limits_{t = 1}^{T}\left\| {y_{t} - {({{Ah_{t}} + c})}} \right\|^{2}}} \\
\text{s.t.} & {{u_{t} = {{Wh_{t - 1}} + {Vx_{t}} + b}},} \\
 & {{{h_{0} = 0},{{h_{t} = {(u_{t})}_{+}},{t = {1,2,\ldots,T}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Problems ([1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S1.E1 "Equation 1 ‣ 1 Introduction ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) and ([3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E3 "Equation 3 ‣ 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) are equivalent in the sense that if $`(A^{\ast},W^{\ast},V^{\ast},b^{\ast},c^{\ast})`$ is a global solution of ([1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S1.E1 "Equation 1 ‣ 1 Introduction ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")), then $`\mathbf{s}^{\ast} = {(\mathbf{z}^{\ast};\mathbf{h}^{\ast};\mathbf{u}^{\ast})}`$ is a global solution of ([3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E3 "Equation 3 ‣ 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) where $`\mathbf{z}^{\ast}`$ is defined by $`(A^{\ast},W^{\ast},V^{\ast},b^{\ast},c^{\ast})`$ and $`\mathbf{h}^{\ast},\mathbf{u}^{\ast}`$ satisfy the constraints of ([3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E3 "Equation 3 ‣ 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) with $`W^{\ast},V^{\ast},b^{\ast}`$. Conversely, if $`\mathbf{s}^{\ast}`$ is a global solution of ([3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E3 "Equation 3 ‣ 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")), then $`\mathbf{z}^{\ast}`$ is a global solution of ([1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S1.E1 "Equation 1 ‣ 1 Introduction ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")).

Let us denote the mappings $`\Phi:{{\mathbb{R}}^{r}\mapsto{\mathbb{R}}^{m \times N_{\mathbf{a}}}}`$ and $`\Psi:{{\mathbb{R}}^{rT}\mapsto{\mathbb{R}}^{{rT} \times N_{\mathbf{w}}}}`$ as

$`{{{\Phi{(h_{t})}} = \begin{bmatrix}
{h_{t}^{\top} \otimes I_{m}} & I_{m}
\end{bmatrix}},{{\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}} = \begin{bmatrix}
{0_{r}^{\top} \otimes I_{r}} & {x_{1}^{\top} \otimes I_{r}} & I_{r} \\
{h_{1}^{\top} \otimes I_{r}} & {x_{2}^{\top} \otimes I_{r}} & I_{r} \\
\vdots & \vdots & \vdots \\
{h_{T - 1}^{\top} \otimes I_{r}} & {x_{T}^{\top} \otimes I_{r}} & I_{r}
\end{bmatrix}}},`$

where $`\otimes`$ represents the Kronecker product, $`I_{r}`$ and $`I_{m}`$ are the identity matrices with dimensions $`r`$ and $`m`$ respectively, and $`0_{r}`$ is the zero vector with dimension $`r`$. Thus, the objective function and constraints in problem [Eq. 3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E3 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") can be represented as

```math
\begin{matrix}
{{{\ell{(\mathbf{s})}}:={\frac{1}{T}{\sum\limits_{t = 1}^{T}\left\| {y_{t} - {\Phi{(h_{t})}\mathbf{a}}} \right\|^{2}}}},} \\
{{{{\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s})}}:={\mathbf{u} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}\mathbf{w}}} = 0},{{\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s})}}:={\mathbf{h} - {(\mathbf{u})}_{+}} = 0}}.}
\end{matrix}
```

To mitigate the overfitting, we further add a regularization term

$`{P{(\mathbf{s})}}:={{\lambda_{1}{\| A\|}_{F}^{2}} + {\lambda_{2}{\| W\|}_{F}^{2}} + {\lambda_{3}{\| V\|}_{F}^{2}} + {\lambda_{4}{\| b\|}^{2}} + {\lambda_{5}{\| c\|}^{2}} + {\lambda_{6}{\|\mathbf{u}\|}^{2}}}`$

with $`{\lambda_{i} > 0},{i = {1,2,\ldots,6}}`$ in the objective of problem [Eq. 3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E3 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), and consider the following problem:

$`\min`$$`{\mathcal{R}{(\mathbf{s})}}:={{\ell{(\mathbf{s})}} + {P{(\mathbf{s})}}}`$$`{\mathbf{s} \in \mathcal{F}:={\{\mathbf{s}:{{{\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s})}} = 0},{{\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s})}} = 0}}\}}}.`$

### 2.2 Optimality conditions

Problem ([3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E3 "Equation 3 ‣ 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) and problem ([7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "Equation 7 ‣ 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) have the same feasible set $`\mathcal{F}.`$ The constraint function $`\mathcal{C}_{1}`$ is continuously differentiable, while the other constraint function $`\mathcal{C}_{2}`$ is linear in $`\mathbf{h}`$ and piecewise linear in $`\mathbf{u}.`$ We denote by $`J\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s})}`$ the Jacobian matrix of the function $`\mathcal{C}_{1}`$ at $`\mathbf{s}`$, and by $`J_{\mathbf{z}}\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s})}`$, $`J_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s})}`$, $`J_{\mathbf{u}}\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s})}`$ the Jacobian matrix of function $`\mathcal{C}_{1}`$ at $`\mathbf{s}`$ with respect to the block $`\mathbf{z}`$, $`\mathbf{h}`$ and $`\mathbf{u}`$, respectively. Similarly, we use $`J_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s})}`$ to represent the Jacobian matrix of $`\mathcal{C}_{2}`$ at $`\mathbf{s}`$ with respect to $`\mathbf{h}`$. Moreover, for a fixed vector $`\zeta \in {\mathbb{R}}^{rT}`$, we use $`\partial\left( {\zeta^{\top}\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s})}} \right)`$ to denote the limiting subdifferential of $`\zeta^{\top}\mathcal{C}_{2}`$ at $`\mathbf{s}`$ and $`\partial_{\mathbf{u}}\left( {\zeta^{\top}\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s})}} \right)`$ to denote the limiting subdifferential of $`\zeta^{\top}\mathcal{C}_{2}`$ at $`\mathbf{s}`$ with respect to $`\mathbf{u}`$.

The following lemma shows that the NNAMCQ \[[29](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib29), Definition 4.2, pp. 1451\] holds at any feasible point $`{\mathbf{s} \in \mathcal{F}}.`$ The proofs of all lemmas are given in [Appendix A](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#A1 "Appendix A Proofs of the lemmas ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

###### Lemma 2.1.

The NNAMCQ holds at any $`\mathbf{s} \in \mathcal{F}`$, i.e., there exist no nonzero vectors $`\xi = {(\xi_{1};\xi_{2};\ldots;\xi_{T})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{rT}`$ and $`\zeta = {(\zeta_{1};\zeta_{2};\ldots;\zeta_{T})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{rT}`$ such that

$`{0 \in {{J\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s})}^{\top}\xi} + {\partial\left( {\zeta^{\top}\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s})}} \right)}}}.`$

###### Definition 2.2.

We say that $`\mathbf{s} \in \mathcal{F}`$ is a KKT point of problem [Eq. 3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E3 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") if there exist $`\xi \in {\mathbb{R}}^{rT}`$ and $`\zeta \in {\mathbb{R}}^{rT}`$ such that

```math
{0 \in {{{\nabla\ell}{(\mathbf{s})}} + {J\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s})}^{\top}\xi} + {\partial\left( {\zeta^{\top}\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s})}} \right)}}}.
```

We say that $`\mathbf{s} \in \mathcal{F}`$ is a KKT point of problem [Eq. 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") if there exist $`\xi \in {\mathbb{R}}^{rT}`$ and $`\zeta \in {\mathbb{R}}^{rT}`$ such that

```math
{0 \in {{{\nabla\mathcal{R}}{(\mathbf{s})}} + {J\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s})}^{\top}\xi} + {\partial\left( {\zeta^{\top}\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s})}} \right)}}}.
```

Now we can establish the first order necessary conditions for problem [Eq. 3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E3 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and problem [Eq. 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

###### Theorem 2.3.

(i) If $`\overline{\mathbf{s}}`$ is a local solution of problem [Eq. 3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E3 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), then $`\overline{\mathbf{s}}`$ is a KKT point of problem [Eq. 3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E3 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). (ii) If $`\overline{\mathbf{s}}`$ is a local solution of problem [Eq. 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), then $`\overline{\mathbf{s}}`$ is a KKT point of problem [Eq. 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

###### Proof 2.4.

Note that the objective functions of problem [Eq. 3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E3 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and problem [Eq. 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") are continuously differentiable. The constraint functions $`\mathcal{C}_{1}`$ is continuously differentiable, and $`\mathcal{C}_{2}`$ is Lipschitz continuous at any feasible point $`\mathbf{s} \in \mathcal{F}`$. By [Lemma 2.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma 2.1. ‣ 2.2 Optimality conditions ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), NNAMCQ holds at any $`\overline{s} \in \mathcal{F}`$. Therefore, the conclusions of this theorem hold according to \[[29](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib29), Remark 2 and Theorem 5.2\].

### 2.3 Nonempty and compact solution set of [Eq. 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")

Let $`\mathcal{S}_{1}`$ be the solution set of problem [Eq. 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), and denote the level set

$`{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{R}}{(\rho)}}:={\{{\mathbf{s} \in \mathcal{F}}:{{\mathcal{R}{(\mathbf{s})}} \leq \rho}\}}`$

with a nonnegative scalar $`\rho`$.

###### Lemma 2.5.

For any $`\rho > {\mathcal{R}{(0)}}`$, the level set $`D_{\mathcal{R}}{(\rho)}`$ is nonempty and compact. Moreover, the solution set $`\mathcal{S}_{1}`$ of [Eq. 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") is nonempty and compact.

## 3 ALM with BCD method for [Eq. 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")

To solve the regularized constrained problem [Eq. 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), we develop in this section an ALM. The subproblems of ALM are approximately solved by a BCD method whose update of each block owns a closed-form expression. This is not an easy task due to the nonsmooth nonconvex constraints. The framework of the ALM is given in [Algorithm 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg1 "In 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), in which the updating schemes for Lagrangian multipliers and penalty parameters are motivated by \[[9](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib9)\]. It is worth mentioning that in \[[9](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib9)\], the constraints are smooth. In problem [Eq. 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), the constraints are nonsmooth nonconvex. For solving the subproblems in the ALM, we design the BCD method in Algorithm [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and provide the closed-form expression for the update of each block in the BCD. Due to the nonsmooth nonconvex constraints in [Eq. 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), the convergence analysis is complex, which will be given in section [4](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4 "4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

The augmented Lagrangian (AL) function of problem [Eq. 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") is

$`\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}`$$`:={{\mathcal{R}{(\mathbf{s})}} + {\langle\xi,{\mathbf{u} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}\mathbf{w}}}\rangle} + {\langle\zeta,{\mathbf{h} - {(\mathbf{u})}_{+}}\rangle} + {\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\| {\mathbf{u} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}\mathbf{w}}} \right\|^{2}} + {\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\| {\mathbf{h} - {(\mathbf{u})}_{+}} \right\|^{2}}}`$$`{= {{{\mathcal{R}{(\mathbf{s})}} + {\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\| {{\mathbf{u} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}\mathbf{w}}} + \frac{\xi}{\gamma}} \right\|^{2}} + {\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\| {{\mathbf{h} - {(\mathbf{u})}_{+}} + \frac{\zeta}{\gamma}} \right\|^{2}}} - \frac{{\|\xi\|}^{2}}{2\gamma} - \frac{{\|\zeta\|}^{2}}{2\gamma}}},`$

where $`\xi = {(\xi_{1};\xi_{2};\ldots;\xi_{T})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{rT}`$ and $`\zeta = {(\zeta_{1};\zeta_{2};\ldots;\zeta_{T})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{rT}`$ are the Lagrangian multipliers, and $`\gamma > 0`$ is the penalty parameter for the two quadratic penalty terms of constraints $`\mathbf{u} = {\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}\mathbf{w}}`$ and $`\mathbf{h} = {(\mathbf{u})}_{+}`$. For convenience, we will also write $`\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{h},\mathbf{u},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}`$ to represent $`\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}`$ when the blocks of $`\mathbf{s}`$ are emphasized.

We develop some basic results in the following two lemmas relating to the AL function $`\mathcal{L}`$. The explicit formulas for the gradients of $`\mathcal{L}`$ with respect to $`\mathbf{z}`$ and $`\mathbf{h}`$ in Lemma [3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") (iii) and (iv) will be used for obtaining the closed-form updates for the $`\mathbf{z}`$ and $`\mathbf{h}`$ blocks in the BCD method, respectively. The Lipschitz constants $`L_{1}{(\xi,\zeta,\gamma,\hat{r})}`$ and $`L_{2}{(\xi,\zeta,\gamma,\hat{r})}`$ in Lemma [3.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.Thmtheorem2 "Lemma 3.2. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") are essential to design a practical stopping condition ([30](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E30 "Equation 30In Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) of the BCD method in Algorithm [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). The results will also be used for the convergence results of the BCD method in Theorems [4.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Thmtheorem3 "Theorem 4.3. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and [4.5](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Thmtheorem5 "Theorem 4.5. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

###### Lemma 3.1.

For any fixed $`\gamma,\xi`$ and $`\zeta`$, the following statements hold.

1.  (i)
    The AL function $`\mathcal{L}`$ is lower bounded that satisfies

    $`{{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} \geq {{{- \frac{{\|\xi\|}^{2}}{2\gamma}} - \frac{{\|\zeta\|}^{2}}{2\gamma}}\quad{\text{for all}\mathbf{s}}}}.`$

2.  (ii)
    For any $`\hat{\mathbf{s}}`$ and $`\hat{\Gamma} \geq \hat{r}:={\mathcal{L}{(\hat{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}`$, the level set

    $`{\Omega_{\mathcal{L}}{(\hat{\Gamma})}}:={\{\mathbf{s}:{{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} \leq \hat{\Gamma}}\}}`$
    is nonempty and compact.

3.  (iii)
    The AL function $`\mathcal{L}`$ is continuously differentiable with respect to $`\mathbf{z}`$, and the gradient with respect to $`\mathbf{z}`$ is

    $`{{{\nabla_{\mathbf{z}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{h},\mathbf{u},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} = \left\lbrack \begin{array}{l}
    {{{\hat{Q}}_{1}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}\mathbf{w}} + {{\hat{q}}_{1}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}} \\
    {{{\hat{Q}}_{2}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}\mathbf{a}} + {{\hat{q}}_{2}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}}
    \end{array} \right\rbrack},`$
    where

    $`{{{\hat{Q}}_{1}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} = {{\gamma\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}^{\top}\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}} + {2\Lambda_{1}}}},{{{\hat{q}}_{1}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} = {- {\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}^{\top}{({\xi + {\gamma\mathbf{u}}})}}}}`$$`{{{\hat{Q}}_{2}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} = {{\frac{2}{T}{\sum\limits_{t = 1}^{T}{\Phi{(h_{t})}^{\top}\Phi{(h_{t})}}}} + {2\Lambda_{2}}}},{{{\hat{q}}_{2}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} = {- {\frac{2}{T}{\sum\limits_{t = 1}^{T}{\Phi{(h_{t})}^{\top}y_{t}}}}}}`$$`{{\Lambda_{1} = {{diag}\left( \left( {\lambda_{2}{\mathbf{e}}_{r^{_{2}}}};{\lambda_{3}{\mathbf{e}}_{rn}};{\lambda_{4}{\mathbf{e}}_{r}} \right) \right)}},{\Lambda_{2} = {{diag}\left( \left( {\lambda_{1}{\mathbf{e}}_{rm}};{\lambda_{5}{\mathbf{e}}_{m}} \right) \right)}}}.`$

4.  (iv)
    The AL function $`\mathcal{L}`$ is continuously differentiable with respect to $`\mathbf{h}`$, and the gradient with respect to $`\mathbf{h}`$ is

    $`{\nabla_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{h},\mathbf{u},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}`$$`=`$$`\left( {{\nabla_{h_{1}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{h},\mathbf{u},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}};{{\nabla_{h_{2}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{h},\mathbf{u},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}};\ldots;{{\nabla_{h_{T}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{h},\mathbf{u},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} \right),`$
    where

    $`{{\nabla_{h_{t}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{h},\mathbf{u},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll}
    {{{D_{1}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}h_{t}} - {d_{1t}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}},} & {{{{if}t} \in {\lbrack{T - 1}\rbrack}},} \\
    {{{D_{2}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}h_{T}} - {d_{2T}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}},} & {{{{if}t} = T},}
    \end{array} \right.`$$`{{D_{1}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} = {{\gamma W^{\top}W} + {\frac{2}{T}A^{\top}A} + {\gamma I_{r}}}},`$$`{{D_{2}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} = {{\frac{2}{T}A^{\top}A} + {\gamma I_{r}}}},`$$`{{d_{1t}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} = {{{{W^{\top}\left( {\xi_{t + 1} + {\gamma{({u_{t + 1} - {Vx_{t + 1}} - b})}}} \right)} + {\gamma{(u_{t})}_{+}}} - \zeta_{t}} + {\frac{2}{T}A^{\top}{({y_{t} - c})}}}},`$$`{{d_{2T}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} = {{{\gamma{(u_{T})}_{+}} - \zeta_{T}} + {\frac{2}{T}A^{\top}{({y_{T} - c})}}}}.`$

###### Lemma 3.2.

For any $`\mathbf{z},\mathbf{h},\mathbf{u},\mathbf{h}',\mathbf{u}'`$ in the level set $`\Omega_{\mathcal{L}}{(\hat{r})}`$, we have

```math
{{\|{{{\nabla_{\mathbf{z}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{h}',\mathbf{u}',\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} - {{\nabla_{\mathbf{z}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{h},\mathbf{u},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}}\|} \leq {L_{1}{(\xi,\zeta,\gamma,\hat{r})}\left\| \begin{array}{l}
{\mathbf{h}' - \mathbf{h}} \\
{\mathbf{u}' - \mathbf{u}}
\end{array} \right\|}},
```

```math
{{\|{{{\nabla_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{h},\mathbf{u}',\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} - {{\nabla_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{h},\mathbf{u},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}}\|} \leq {L_{2}{(\xi,\zeta,\gamma,\hat{r})}\left\| {\mathbf{u}' - \mathbf{u}} \right\|}},
```

where

$`{{{L_{1}{(\xi,\zeta,\gamma,\hat{r})}} = {\sqrt{2}{\max{\{{\gamma\delta_{1}},{\delta_{2} + \delta_{3} + \delta_{4}}\}}}}},{{L_{2}{(\xi,\zeta,\gamma,\hat{r})}} = {\gamma\delta_{5}}}},`$

with $`X:={(x_{1};x_{2};\ldots;x_{T})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{nT}`$,

$`{{\delta = {\hat{r} + \frac{{\|\xi\|}^{2}}{2\gamma} + \frac{{\|\zeta\|}^{2}}{2\gamma}}},{{\delta_{0} = {\sqrt{\frac{2\delta}{\gamma}} + \sqrt{\frac{\delta}{\lambda_{6}}} + \frac{\|\zeta\|}{\gamma}}},{\delta_{1} = \sqrt{r{({\delta^{2} + {\| X\|}^{2} + T})}}}}},`$$`{{\delta_{2} = {2\gamma\delta_{1}\sqrt{\frac{r\delta}{\min{\{\lambda_{2},\lambda_{3},\lambda_{4}\}}}}}},{\delta_{3} = {{\sqrt{r}{\|\xi\|}} + {\gamma\sqrt{\frac{r\delta}{\lambda_{6}}}}}}},`$$`{{\delta_{4} = {\frac{2\sqrt{m}}{\sqrt{T}}\left( {{2\sqrt{m{({\delta_{0}^{2} + 1})}}\sqrt{\frac{\delta}{\min{\{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{5}\}}}}} + {\max\limits_{1 \leq t \leq T}{\| y_{t}\|}}} \right)}},{\delta_{5} = {\sqrt{\frac{\delta{({T - 1})}}{\lambda_{2}}} + \sqrt{T}}}}.`$

### 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs

To solve the regularized constrained problem ([7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "Equation 7 ‣ 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")), we propose the ALM in Algorithm [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). The ALM first approximately solves ([18](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E18 "Equation 18In Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) that aims to minimize the AL function with the fixed Lagrange multipliers $`\xi^{k - 1}`$ and $`\zeta^{k - 1}`$, and the fixed penalty parameter $`\gamma_{k - 1}`$ for the quadratic terms, until $`\mathbf{s}^{k}`$ satisfies the approximate first-order optimality necessary condition [Eq. 19](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E19 "In 3 ‣ Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") with tolerance $`\epsilon_{k - 1}`$. Then the Lagrange multipliers are updated, and the tolerance $`\epsilon_{k}`$ is reduced so that in the next iteration the subproblem is solved more accurately. Moreover, the penalty parameter $`\gamma_{k}`$ is unchanged if the feasibility of $`\mathbf{s}^{k}`$ is sufficiently improved compared to that of $`\mathbf{s}^{k - 1}`$, otherwise, $`\gamma_{k}`$ is increased.

1:  Set an initial penalty parameter $`\gamma_{0} > 0`$, parameters $`{\eta_{1},\eta_{2},\eta_{4}} \in {(0,1)}`$ and $`\eta_{3} > 1`$, an initial tolerance $`\epsilon_{0} > 0`$, vectors of Lagrangian multipliers $`\xi^{0}`$, $`\zeta^{0}`$, and a feasible initial point $`\mathbf{s}^{0} = {(\mathbf{z}^{0},\hat{\mathbf{h}},\hat{\mathbf{u}})}`$ where $`{\hat{h}}_{0} = 0`$, $`{\hat{u}}_{t} = {{W{\hat{h}}_{t - 1}} + {Vx_{t}} + b}`$ and $`{\hat{h}}_{t} = {({\hat{u}}_{t})}_{+}`$ for $`t \in {\lbrack T\rbrack}`$.

2:  Set $`k:=1`$.

3:  Step 1: Solve $`\min\limits_{\mathbf{s}}\mspace{21mu}`$$`\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s},\xi^{k - 1},\zeta^{k - 1},\gamma_{k - 1})}`$ to obtain $`\mathbf{s}^{k}`$ satisfying the following condition $`{{\text{dist}\left( 0,{\partial{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{k},\xi^{k - 1},\zeta^{k - 1},\gamma_{k - 1})}}} \right)} \leq \epsilon_{k - 1}}.`$

4:  Step 2: Update $`\epsilon_{k} = {\eta_{4}\epsilon_{k - 1}}`$, $`\xi^{k - 1}`$ and $`\zeta^{k - 1}`$ as $`{{\xi^{k} = {\xi^{k - 1} + {\gamma_{k - 1}\left( {\mathbf{u}^{k} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h}^{k})}\mathbf{w}^{k}}} \right)}}},{\zeta^{k} = {\zeta^{k - 1} + {\gamma_{k - 1}\left( {\mathbf{h}^{k} - {(\mathbf{u}^{k})}_{+}} \right)}}}}.`$

5:  Step 3: Set $`\gamma_{k} = \gamma_{k - 1}`$, if the following condition is satisfied $`{{\max\left\{ {\|{\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s}^{k})}}\|},{\|{\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s}^{k})}}\|} \right\}} \leq {\eta_{1}{\max\left\{ {\|{\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s}^{k - 1})}}\|},{\|{\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s}^{k - 1})}}\|} \right\}}}}.`$

6:  Otherwise, set $`{\gamma_{k} = {\max\left\{ {\gamma_{k - 1}/\eta_{2}},\left\| \xi^{k} \right\|^{1 + \eta_{3}},\left\| \zeta^{k} \right\|^{1 + \eta_{3}} \right\}}}.`$

7:  Let $`{k - 1}:=k`$ and go to Step 1.

Algorithm 1 The augmented Lagrangian method (ALM) for [Eq. 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")

###### Remark 3.3.

The main operation of Algorithm [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") is to approximately solve the subproblem ([18](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E18 "Equation 18In Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")). Furthermore, to show that Algorithm [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") is well-defined requires that the algorithm for solving the subproblem ([18](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E18 "Equation 18In Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) can be terminated within finite steps to meet the stopping condition in ([19](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E19 "Equation 19In Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")).

In section [3.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.SS2 "3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), we will design a BCD method to solve the subproblem ([18](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E18 "Equation 18In Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")). The update of each block of the BCD method owns a closed-form formula, which makes the BCD method efficient. Moreover, the stopping condition ([19](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E19 "Equation 19In Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) can be replaced by a simpler condition ([30](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E30 "Equation 30In Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) as will be shown in Theorem [4.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Thmtheorem3 "Theorem 4.3. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

### 3.2 BCD method for subproblem

To solve the nonsmooth nonconvex problem ([18](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E18 "Equation 18In Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) in Step 1 of [Algorithm 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg1 "In 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), we propose a BCD method in [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") to solve the subproblem at the $`k`$-th iteration in the ALM by alternatively updating the blocks in the order of $`\mathbf{z}`$, $`\mathbf{h}`$, and $`\mathbf{u}`$ in $`\mathbf{s}`$, respectively. Let us choose a constant $`\Gamma`$ such that

$`{\Gamma \geq {\mathcal{L}\left( \mathbf{s}^{0},\xi^{0},\zeta^{0},\gamma_{0} \right)}}.`$

Because at the $`k`$-th iteration of the ALM, $`\xi^{k - 1},\zeta^{k - 1},\gamma_{k - 1}`$ are fixed, we just write $`\xi,\zeta,\gamma`$ in the BCD method for brevity. Furthermore, for the BCD solving the subproblem appeared at the $`k`$-th iteration of the ALM, we define

$`{\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{{k - 1},j}:={(\mathbf{z}^{{k - 1},j};\mathbf{h}^{{k - 1},{j - 1}};\mathbf{u}^{{k - 1},{j - 1}})}},{\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{{k - 1},j}:={(\mathbf{z}^{{k - 1},j};\mathbf{h}^{{k - 1},j};\mathbf{u}^{{k - 1},{j - 1}})}}`$

to denote the point obtained after updating the $`\mathbf{z}`$ block, and updating the $`\mathbf{h}`$ block at the $`j`$-th iteration of the BCD method, and we use

$`\mathbf{s}^{{k - 1},j} = {(\mathbf{z}^{{k - 1},j};\mathbf{h}^{{k - 1},j};\mathbf{u}^{{k - 1},j})}`$

to represent the point obtained at the $`j`$-th iteration of the BCD method after updating the $`\mathbf{u}`$ block.

1:  Set the initial point of BCD algorithm as $`\mathbf{s}^{{k - 1},0} = \begin{cases}
{\mathbf{s}^{k - 1},} & {{{\text{if}k} > {1\text{and}\begin{matrix}
{{\mathcal{L}\left( \mathbf{s}^{k - 1},\xi,\zeta,\gamma \right)} \leq \Gamma}
\end{matrix}}},} \\
{\mathbf{s}^{0},} & \text{otherwise.}
\end{cases}`$ Compute $`{\hat{r}}_{k - 1} = {\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{{k - 1},0},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}`$, $`L_{1,{k - 1}} = {L_{1}{(\xi,\zeta,\gamma,{\hat{r}}_{k - 1})}}`$ and $`L_{2,{k - 1}} = {L_{2}{(\xi,\zeta,\gamma,{\hat{r}}_{k - 1})}}`$ by formula ([17](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E17 "Equation 17 ‣ Lemma 3.2. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")).

2:  Set $`j:=1`$.

3:  while the stop criterion is not met do

4:     Step 1: Update blocks $`\mathbf{z}^{{k - 1},j}`$, $`\mathbf{h}^{{k - 1},j}`$ and $`\mathbf{u}^{{k - 1},j}`$ separately as $`\mathbf{z}^{{k - 1},j}`$$`{= {{\arg{\min\limits_{\mathbf{z}}\mathcal{L}}}\left( \mathbf{z},\mathbf{h}^{{k - 1},{j - 1}},\mathbf{u}^{{k - 1},{j - 1}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma \right)}},`$$`\mathbf{h}^{{k - 1},j}`$$`{= {{\arg{\min\limits_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{L}}}\left( \mathbf{z}^{{k - 1},j},\mathbf{h},\mathbf{u}^{{k - 1},{j - 1}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma \right)}},`$$`\mathbf{u}^{{k - 1},j}`$$`{\in {{{\arg{\min\limits_{\mathbf{u}}\mathcal{L}}}\left( \mathbf{z}^{{k - 1},j},\mathbf{h}^{{k - 1},j},\mathbf{u},\xi,\zeta,\gamma \right)} + {\frac{\mu}{2}\left\| {\mathbf{u} - \mathbf{u}^{{k - 1},{j - 1}}} \right\|^{2}}}}.`$ Then set $`\mathbf{s}^{{k - 1},j} = {(\mathbf{z}^{{k - 1},j};\mathbf{h}^{{k - 1},j};\mathbf{u}^{{k - 1},j})}`$.

5:     Step 2: If the stop criterion $`{\left\| {\mathbf{s}^{{k - 1},j} - \mathbf{s}^{{k - 1},{j - 1}}} \right\| \leq \frac{\epsilon_{k - 1}}{\max{\{ L_{1,{k - 1}},L_{2,{k - 1}},\mu\}}}},`$ is not satisfied, then set $`j:={j + 1}`$ and go to Step 1.

6:  end while

7:  return  $`\mathbf{s}^{k} = \mathbf{s}^{{k - 1},j}`$.

Algorithm 2 Block Coordinate Descent (BCD) method for [Eq. 18](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E18 "In 3 ‣ Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")

Condition ([19](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E19 "Equation 19In Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) is satisfied when ([30](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E30 "Equation 30In Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) holds, which will be proved in Theorem [4.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Thmtheorem3 "Theorem 4.3. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). The closed-form solutions of problems [Eq. 27](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E27 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), [Eq. 28](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E28 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and [Eq. 29](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E29 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") are provided below.

Update $`\mathbf{z}^{{k - 1},j}`$: Problem [Eq. 27](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E27 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") is an unconstrained optimization problem with smooth and strongly convex objective function. By employing [Lemma 3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") [(iii)](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.I1.i3 "Item (iii) ‣ Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and solving

$`{{{\nabla_{\mathbf{z}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{{k - 1},j},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} = 0},`$

the unique global minimizer $`\mathbf{z}^{{k - 1},j} = {(\mathbf{w}^{{k - 1},j};\mathbf{a}^{{k - 1},j})}`$ can be computed as

$`{\mathbf{w}^{{k - 1},j} = {- {{\hat{Q}}_{1}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{{k - 1},j},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}^{- 1}{\hat{q}}_{1}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{{k - 1},j};\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}}},`$$`{\mathbf{a}^{{k - 1},j} = {- {{\hat{Q}}_{2}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{{k - 1},j},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}^{- 1}{\hat{q}}_{2}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{{k - 1},j},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}}}.`$

Update $`\mathbf{h}^{{k - 1},j}`$: The objective function of [Eq. 28](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E28 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") is also strongly convex and smooth. By employing [Lemma 3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") [(iv)](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.I1.i4 "Item (iv) ‣ Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and solving $`{{{\nabla_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{{k - 1},j},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} = 0},`$ we get its unique global minimizer, given by

```math
\begin{array}{r}
{h_{t}^{{k - 1},j} = \begin{cases}
{{D_{1}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{{k - 1},j},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}^{- 1}d_{1t}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{{k - 1},j},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}},} & {{{\text{if}t} \in {\lbrack{T - 1}\rbrack}},} \\
{{D_{2}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{{k - 1},j},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}^{- 1}d_{2T}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{{k - 1},j},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}},} & {{{\text{if}t} = T}.}
\end{cases}}
\end{array}
```

Update $`\mathbf{u}^{{k - 1},j}`$: Although problem [Eq. 29](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E29 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") is nonsmooth nonconvex, one of its global solutions is accessible, because the objective function of problem [Eq. 29](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E29 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") can be separated into $`rT`$ one-dimensional functions with the same structure. Thus, we aim to solve the following one-dimensional problem:

```math
{{\min\limits_{u \in {\mathbb{R}}}{\varphi{(u)}}}:={{\frac{\gamma}{2}{({u - \theta_{1}})}^{2}} + {\frac{\gamma}{2}{({\theta_{2} - {(u)}_{+}})}^{2}} + {\frac{\mu}{2}{({u - \theta_{3}})}^{2}} + {\lambda_{6}u^{2}}}},
```

where $`{\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\theta_{3}} \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ are known real numbers. Denote

$`{{u^{+}:={{\underset{u \in {\mathbb{R}}_{+}}{\arg\min}{\varphi{(u)}}}\quad\text{and}}}\quad{u^{-}:={\underset{u \in {\mathbb{R}}_{-}}{\arg\min}{\varphi{(u)}}}}}.`$

By direct computation,

$`u^{+} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll}
{\frac{{\gamma\theta_{1}} + {\gamma\theta_{2}} + {\mu\theta_{3}}}{{2\gamma} + {2\lambda_{6}} + \mu},} & {{{{\text{if}\gamma\theta_{1}} + {\gamma\theta_{2}} + {\mu\theta_{3}}} > 0},} \\
{0,} & {\text{otherwise},}
\end{array} \right.`$

and

$`u^{-} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll}
{\frac{{\gamma\theta_{1}} + {\mu\theta_{3}}}{\gamma + {2\lambda_{6}} + \mu},} & {{{{\text{if}\gamma\theta_{1}} + {\mu\theta_{3}}} < 0},} \\
{0,} & {\text{otherwise}.}
\end{array} \right.`$

Then a solution of ([32](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E32 "Equation 32 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) can be given as

$`u^{\ast} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll}
{u^{+},} & {{{\text{if}\varphi{(u^{+})}} \leq {\varphi{(u^{-})}}},} \\
{u^{-},} & {\text{otherwise}.}
\end{array} \right.`$

By setting

$`{{\theta_{1} = {{({\Psi{(\mathbf{h}^{{k - 1},j})}\mathbf{w}^{{k - 1},j}})}_{i} - \frac{\xi_{i}}{\gamma}}},{{\theta_{2} = {\mathbf{h}_{i}^{{k - 1},j} + \frac{\zeta_{i}}{\gamma}}},{\theta_{3} = \mathbf{u}_{i}^{{k - 1},{j - 1}}}}},`$$`{{\mathbf{u}_{i}^{{k - 1},j} = u^{\ast}},{{\mathbf{u}_{i}^{+} = u^{+}},{\mathbf{u}_{i}^{-} = u^{-}}}},`$

we obtain a closed-form solution of problem [Eq. 29](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E29 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") as

$`\mathbf{u}_{i}^{{k - 1},j} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll}
{\mathbf{u}_{i}^{+},} & {{{\text{if}\varphi{(\mathbf{u}_{i}^{+})}} \leq {\varphi{(\mathbf{u}_{i}^{-})}}},} \\
{\mathbf{u}_{i}^{-},} & {{{{\text{otherwise},i} = 1},{\ldots,{rT}}}.}
\end{array} \right.`$

###### Remark 3.4.

It is important to mention that the solution set of problem [Eq. 29](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E29 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") may not be a singleton. To ensure the selected solution is unique, we set $`\mathbf{u}_{i}^{{k - 1},j} = \mathbf{u}_{i}^{+}`$ when $`{\varphi{(\mathbf{u}_{i}^{+})}} = {\varphi{(\mathbf{u}_{i}^{-})}}`$ for every $`i \in {\lbrack{rT}\rbrack}`$.

## 4 Convergence analysis

In this section, we show the convergence results of both the BCD method for the subproblem of the ALM, as well as the ALM for [Eq. 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

### 4.1 Convergence analysis of [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")

It is clear that

$`{{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} = {{g{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\gamma)}} + {q{(\mathbf{s},\zeta,\gamma)}}}},`$

where

```math
{{g{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\gamma)}} = {{{\mathcal{R}{(\mathbf{s})}} + {\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\| {{\mathbf{u} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}\mathbf{w}}} + \frac{\xi}{\gamma}} \right\|^{2}}} - \frac{{\|\xi\|}^{2}}{2\gamma}}},
```

```math
{{q{(\mathbf{s},\zeta,\gamma)}} = {{\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\| {{\mathbf{h} - {(\mathbf{u})}_{+}} + \frac{\zeta}{\gamma}} \right\|^{2}} - \frac{{\|\zeta\|}^{2}}{2\gamma}}}.
```

The function $`g`$ is smooth but nonconvex, because it contains the bilinear structure $`\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}\mathbf{w}`$. The function $`q`$ is nonsmooth nonconvex.

For the convergence analysis below, we further use $`\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(j)}`$ and $`\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(j)}`$ to represent $`\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{{k - 1},j}`$ and $`\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{{k - 1},j}`$ in ([24](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E24 "Equation 24 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")), and use $`\mathbf{s}^{(j)}`$ to represent $`s^{{k - 1},j}`$ in ([25](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E25 "Equation 25 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) for brevity. We emphasize that the point $`\mathbf{s}^{k}`$ is generated by the ALM in [Algorithm 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg1 "In 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), while the point $`\mathbf{s}^{(j)}`$ is generated by the BCD method in [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") for solving the subproblem in the ALM at the $`k`$-th iteration.

The following two lemmas will be used in proving the convergence results of the BCD method.

###### Lemma 4.1.

Let $`\{\mathbf{s}^{(j)}\}`$ represent the sequence generated by [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). Then $`\{\mathbf{s}^{(j)}\}`$ belongs to the level set $`\Omega_{\mathcal{L}}{(\Gamma)}`$, which is compact.

###### Lemma 4.2.

The AL function $`\mathcal{L}`$ is locally Lipschitz continuous and directionally differentiable on $`{\Omega_{\mathcal{L}}{(\Gamma)}}.`$

We can now show that the stop criterion [Eq. 30](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E30 "In 5 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") in [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") can be stopped in finite steps, and condition ([19](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E19 "Equation 19In Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) in Algorithm [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") is satisfied when ([30](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E30 "Equation 30In Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) holds.
