These results guarantee that the ALM in Algorithm [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") is well-defined, when the subproblems are solved by the BCD method in [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

###### Theorem 4.3.

At the $`k`$-th iteration of ALM in [Algorithm 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg1 "In 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), the BCD method in [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") for the subproblem [Eq. 18](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E18 "In 3 ‣ Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") can be stopped within finite steps to satisfy the stop criterion in [Eq. 30](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E30 "In 5 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), which is of order $`O{({1/{(\epsilon_{k - 1})}^{2}})}`$. Moreover, condition [Eq. 19](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E19 "In 3 ‣ Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") of the ALM in [Algorithm 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg1 "In 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") is satisfied at the output $`\mathbf{s}^{k}`$ of [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

###### Proof 4.4.

Since $`\mathcal{L}`$ is strongly convex with respect to the blocks $`\mathbf{z}`$ and $`\mathbf{h}`$, respectively, from [Eq. 27](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E27 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and [Eq. 28](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E28 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), we obtain

$`{{{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{({j - 1})},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} - {\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}} \geq {\frac{\alpha_{1}}{2}{\|{\mathbf{z}^{({j - 1})} - \mathbf{z}^{(j)}}\|}^{2}}},`$$`{{{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} - {\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}} \geq {\frac{\alpha_{2}}{2}{\|{\mathbf{h}^{({j - 1})} - \mathbf{h}^{(j)}}\|}^{2}}},`$

where $`\alpha_{1}`$ and $`\alpha_{2}`$ are the minimum eigenvalues of the Hessian matrices $`{\nabla_{\mathbf{z}}^{2}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}`$ and $`{\nabla_{\mathbf{h}}^{2}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}`$ for all s in the compact set $`\Omega_{\mathcal{L}}{(\Gamma)}`$, respectively. Furthermore, by [Eq. 29](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E29 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), we have

$`{{{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} - {\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}} \geq {\frac{\mu}{2}\left\| {\mathbf{u}^{(j)} - \mathbf{u}^{({j - 1})}} \right\|^{2}}}.`$

It follows that

$`{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{({j - 1})},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} - {\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}`$$`=`$$`\left( {{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{({j - 1})},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} - {\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}} \right) + \left( {{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} - {\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}} \right)`$$`+ \left( {{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} - {\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}} \right)`$$`\geq`$$`{\frac{\alpha_{1}}{2}{\|{\mathbf{z}^{(j)} - \mathbf{z}^{({j - 1})}}\|}^{2}} + {\frac{\alpha_{2}}{2}{\|{\mathbf{h}^{(j)} - \mathbf{h}^{({j - 1})}}\|}^{2}} + {\frac{\mu}{2}{\|{\mathbf{u}^{(j)} - \mathbf{u}^{({j - 1})}}\|}^{2}}`$$`\geq`$$`{{\max{\{\frac{\alpha_{1}}{2},\frac{\alpha_{2}}{2},\frac{\mu}{2}\}}}{\|{\mathbf{s}^{(j)} - \mathbf{s}^{({j - 1})}}\|}^{2}}.`$

Summing up $`\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{({j - 1})},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}`$ $`-`$ $`\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}`$ from $`j = 1`$ to $`J`$, we have

$`{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{(0)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} - {\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{(J)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}`$$`\geq`$$`{\max{\{\frac{\alpha_{1}}{2},\frac{\alpha_{2}}{2},\frac{\mu}{2}\}}}{\sum\limits_{j = 1}^{J}{\|{\mathbf{s}^{(j)} - \mathbf{s}^{({j - 1})}}\|}^{2}}`$$`\geq`$$`{J{\max{\{\frac{\alpha_{1}}{2},\frac{\alpha_{2}}{2},\frac{\mu}{2}\}}}{\min\limits_{j \in {\lbrack J\rbrack}}{\{{\|{\mathbf{s}^{(j)} - \mathbf{s}^{({j - 1})}}\|}^{2}\}}}}.`$

This, together with [Lemma 3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") [(i)](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.I1.i1 "Item (i) ‣ Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), yields that

$`{{\min\limits_{j \in {\lbrack J\rbrack}}{\{{\|{\mathbf{s}^{(j)} - \mathbf{s}^{({j - 1})}}\|}^{2}\}}} \leq \frac{{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{(0)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} + \frac{{\|\xi\|}^{2}}{2\gamma} + \frac{{\|\zeta\|}^{2}}{2\gamma}}{J{\max{\{\frac{\alpha_{1}}{2},\frac{\alpha_{2}}{2},\frac{\mu}{2}\}}}}}.`$

It follows that the stop criterion [Eq. 30](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E30 "In 5 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") holds, as long as

$`{J \geq \hat{J}:=\left\lceil \frac{\left( {{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{(0)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} + \frac{{\|\xi\|}^{2}}{2\gamma} + \frac{{\|\zeta\|}^{2}}{2\gamma}} \right){({\max{\{ L_{1,{k - 1}},L_{2,{k - 1}},\mu\}}})}^{2}}{{\max{\{\frac{\alpha_{1}}{2},\frac{\alpha_{2}}{2},\frac{\mu}{2}\}}}{(\epsilon_{k - 1})}^{2}} \right\rceil}.`$

Therefore, at the $`k`$-th iteration of the ALM in [Algorithm 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg1 "In 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), the BCD method in [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") can be stopped in at most $`\hat{J}`$ iterations defined in ([49](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E49 "Equation 49 ‣ Proof 4.4. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) and output $`\mathbf{s}^{k}`$, which is of order $`O{({1/{(\epsilon_{k - 1})}^{2}})}`$.

Once condition [Eq. 30](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E30 "In 5 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") is satisfied, condition [Eq. 19](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E19 "In 3 ‣ Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") in [Algorithm 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg1 "In 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") also holds, which will be proved in the following. By Step 1 in [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), the first order optimality condition of the three blocked subproblems [Eq. 27](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E27 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), [Eq. 28](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E28 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and [Eq. 29](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E29 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") are

```math
{{0 = {{\nabla_{\mathbf{z}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}},{0 = {{\nabla_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}}},
```

```math
{0 \in {{{\nabla_{\mathbf{u}}g}{(\mathbf{s}^{(j)},\xi,\gamma)}} + {\partial_{\mathbf{u}}{q{(\mathbf{s}^{(j)},\zeta,\gamma)}}} + {\mu{({\mathbf{u}^{(j)} - \mathbf{u}^{({j - 1})}})}}}}.
```

Furthermore, the limiting subdifferential of the function $`\mathcal{L}`$ at $`\mathbf{s}^{(j)}`$ can be written as

$`{{\partial{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}} = \left( {{\nabla_{\mathbf{z}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{s}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}};{{\nabla_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{s}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}};{{{\nabla_{\mathbf{u}}g}{(\mathbf{s}^{(j)},\xi)}} + {\partial_{\mathbf{u}}{q{(\mathbf{s}^{(j)},\zeta)}}}} \right)}.`$

Hence

```math
{\begin{bmatrix}
{{{\nabla_{\mathbf{z}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{s}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} - {{\nabla_{\mathbf{z}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}} \\
{{{\nabla_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{s}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} - {{\nabla_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}} \\
{- {\mu{({\mathbf{u}^{(j)} - \mathbf{u}^{({j - 1})}})}}}
\end{bmatrix} \in {\partial{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}}}.
```

By [Lemma 3.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.Thmtheorem2 "Lemma 3.2. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), we obtain

$`{dist}\left( 0,{\partial{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}} \right)`$$`\leq`$$`\left\| \begin{matrix}
{{{\nabla_{\mathbf{z}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{s}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} - {{\nabla_{\mathbf{z}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}} \\
{{{\nabla_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{s}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} - {{\nabla_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}} \\
{- {\mu{({\mathbf{u}^{(j)} - \mathbf{u}^{({j - 1})}})}}}
\end{matrix} \right\|`$$`\leq`$$`{{\max{\{ L_{1,{k - 1}},L_{2,{k - 1}},\mu\}}}{\|{\mathbf{s}^{(j)} - \mathbf{s}^{({j - 1})}}\|}}.`$

Thus condition [Eq. 30](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E30 "In 5 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") that $`{\|{\mathbf{s}^{(j)} - \mathbf{s}^{({j - 1})}}\|} \leq {\epsilon_{k - 1}/{\max{\{ L_{1,{k - 1}},L_{2,{k - 1}},\mu\}}}}`$, together with $`\mathbf{s}^{k} = \mathbf{s}^{(j)}`$, implies $`{dist}{(0,{\partial{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{(k)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}})}`$ $`\leq`$ $`\epsilon_{k - 1}`$ in condition [Eq. 19](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E19 "In 3 ‣ Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

Theorem [4.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Thmtheorem3 "Theorem 4.3. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") above guarantees that the BCD method in Algorithm [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") terminates within finite steps to meet the stop criterion ([30](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E30 "Equation 30In Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) for a fixed $`\epsilon_{k - 1} > 0`$. In the rest of this subsection, we discuss the convergence of [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") for the case $`\epsilon_{k - 1} = 0`$, i.e., we replace the stop criterion ([30](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E30 "Equation 30In Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) by

```math
{\left\| {\mathbf{s}^{{k - 1},j} - \mathbf{s}^{{k - 1},{j - 1}}} \right\| = 0}.
```

We will show in Theorem [4.8](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Thmtheorem8 "Theorem 4.8. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") that the BCD method converges to a d-stationary point if $`\epsilon_{k - 1} = 0`$. For this purpose, we first show the following theorem that provides the convergence of the sequences of the function values $`\mathcal{L}`$ with respect to the three blocks, as well as the convergence of the subsequences of the iterative points with respect to the three blocks.

###### Theorem 4.5.

Suppose that ([30](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E30 "Equation 30In Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) is replaced by ([51](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E51 "Equation 51 ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) in [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). If there is $`\overline{j}`$ such that ([51](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E51 "Equation 51 ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) holds, then

```math
{{{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(\overline{j})},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} = {\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(\overline{j})},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} = {\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{(\overline{j})},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}}\quad{{{and}\quad\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(\overline{j})}} = \mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(\overline{j})} = \mathbf{s}^{(\overline{j})}}}.
```

Otherwise, [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") generates infinite sequences $`\{\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(j)}\}`$, $`\{\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(j)}\}`$ and $`\{\mathbf{s}^{(j)}\}`$, and the following statements hold.

1.  (i)
    The sequences $`\{{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}\}`$, $`\{{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}\}`$ and $`\{{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}\}`$ all converge to a constant $`\mathcal{L}^{\ast}`$.
2.  (ii)
    There exists a subsequence $`{\{ j_{i}\}} \subseteq {\{ j\}}`$ such that $`\{\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(j_{i})}\}`$, $`\{\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(j_{i})}\}`$ and $`\{\mathbf{s}^{(j_{i})}\}`$ converging to the same point.

###### Proof 4.6.

If there is $`\overline{j}`$ such that ([51](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E51 "Equation 51 ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) holds, then ([52](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E52 "Equation 52 ‣ Theorem 4.5. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) is derived directly from $`\mathbf{s}^{{k - 1},\overline{j}} = \mathbf{s}^{{k - 1},{\overline{j} - 1}}`$ and ([27](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E27 "Equation 27In Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."))-([29](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E29 "Equation 29In Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")).

If there is no $`\overline{j}`$ such that ([51](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E51 "Equation 51 ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) holds, then [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") generates infinite sequences $`\{\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(j)}\}`$, $`\{\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(j)}\}`$ and $`\{\mathbf{s}^{(j)}\}`$.

[(i)](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.I1.i1 "Item (i) ‣ Theorem 4.5. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") By [Lemma 4.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma 4.1. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), there exists an infinite subsequence $`{\{ j_{i}\}} \subseteq {\{ j\}}`$ such that $`\mathbf{s}^{(j_{i})}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbf{s}}`$ as $`j_{i}\rightarrow\infty`$. Let $`\mathcal{L}^{\ast} = {\mathcal{L}{(\overline{\mathbf{s}})}}`$. We can easily deduce that statement [(i)](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.I1.i1 "Item (i) ‣ Theorem 4.5. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") holds, by the descent inequality [Eq. 101](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#A1.E101 "In Proof A.5. ‣ A.5 Proof of Lemma 4.1 ‣ Appendix A Proofs of the lemmas ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and the lower boundedness of $`\{{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}\}`$ according to [Lemma 3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") [(i)](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.I1.i1 "Item (i) ‣ Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

[(ii)](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.I1.i2 "Item (ii) ‣ Theorem 4.5. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") To further prove that $`\{\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(j_{i})}\}`$ and $`\{\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(j_{i})}\}`$ also converge to $`\overline{\mathbf{s}}`$, it is sufficient to prove

```math
{{{\lim\limits_{i\rightarrow\infty}{\|{\mathbf{s}^{(j_{i})} - \mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(j_{i})}}\|}} = 0},{{\lim\limits_{i\rightarrow\infty}{\|{\mathbf{s}^{(j_{i})} - \mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(j_{i})}}\|}} = 0}}.
```

Letting $`J`$ go to infinity and replacing $`(j)`$ in ([4.4](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Ex44 "Proof 4.4. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) by $`(j_{i})`$, it is easy to have that $`{\sum_{i = 1}^{\infty}{\|{\mathbf{s}^{(j_{i})} - \mathbf{s}^{({j_{i} - 1})}}\|}^{2}} < \infty`$. Hence,

$`{{\lim\limits_{i\rightarrow\infty}{\|{\mathbf{s}^{(j_{i})} - \mathbf{s}^{({j_{i} - 1})}}\|}} = 0},`$

which together with

```math
{{\|{\mathbf{s}^{(j_{i})} - \mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(j_{i})}}\|} \leq {{\|{\mathbf{h}^{(j_{i})} - \mathbf{h}^{({j_{i} - 1})}}\|} + {\|{\mathbf{u}^{(j_{i})} - \mathbf{u}^{({j_{i} - 1})}}\|}}},
```

```math
{{\|{\mathbf{s}^{(j_{i})} - \mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(j_{i})}}\|} \leq {\|{\mathbf{u}^{(j_{i})} - \mathbf{u}^{({j_{i} - 1})}}\|}},
```

implies the validity of ([53](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E53 "Equation 53 ‣ Proof 4.6. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")).

Now we turn to show that [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") generates a d-stationary point of problem [Eq. 18](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E18 "In 3 ‣ Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). For convenience, when considering the directional derivative of a function with respect to a direction and we want to emphasize the blocks of the direction, we adopt a simple expression. For example, if $`d = {(d_{\mathbf{z}};d_{h};d_{\mathbf{u}})}`$, we also write $`{\mathcal{L}'{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma;d)}} = {\mathcal{L}'{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma;{(d_{\mathbf{z}},d_{\mathbf{h}},d_{\mathbf{u}})})}}`$ instead of $`{\mathcal{L}'{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma;{(d_{\mathbf{z}};d_{\mathbf{h}};d_{\mathbf{u}})})}}.`$

###### Lemma 4.7.

If the directional derivatives of $`\mathcal{L}`$ at $`\overline{\mathbf{s}} \in {\Omega_{\mathcal{L}}{(\Gamma)}}`$ satisfy

```math
{{{\mathcal{L}'\left( \overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma;{(d_{\mathbf{z}},0,0)} \right)} \geq 0},{{{\mathcal{L}'\left( \overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma;{(0,d_{\mathbf{h}},0)} \right)} \geq 0},{{\mathcal{L}'\left( \overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma;{(0,0,d_{\mathbf{u}})} \right)} \geq 0}}},
```

along any $`d_{\mathbf{z}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{N_{\mathbf{w}} + N_{\mathbf{a}}}`$, $`d_{\mathbf{h}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{rT}`$ and $`d_{\mathbf{u}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{rT}`$, then

```math
{{{\mathcal{L}'{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma;d)}} \geq 0},{{\forall d} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{N_{\mathbf{w}} + N_{\mathbf{a}} + {2rT}}}}.
```

As problem [Eq. 18](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E18 "In 3 ‣ Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") is nonsmooth nonconvex, there are many kinds of stationary points for it, such as a Fréchet stationary point, a limiting stationary point, and a d-stationary point. It is known that a limiting stationary point is a Fréchet stationary point, and a d-stationary point is a limiting stationary point, but not vise versa \[[19](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib19)\]. The theorem below guarantees that either the BCD method terminates at a d-stationary point of problem [Eq. 18](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E18 "In 3 ‣ Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") in finite steps, or any accumulation point of the sequence generated by the BCD method is a d-stationary point of problem [Eq. 18](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E18 "In 3 ‣ Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

###### Theorem 4.8.

Suppose that ([30](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E30 "Equation 30In Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) is replaced by ([51](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E51 "Equation 51 ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) in [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). If there is $`\overline{j}`$ such that ([51](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E51 "Equation 51 ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) holds, then $`\mathbf{s}^{(\overline{j})}`$ is a d-stationary point of problem [Eq. 18](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E18 "In 3 ‣ Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). Otherwise, [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") generates an infinite sequence $`\{\mathbf{s}^{(j)}\}`$ and any accumulation point of $`\{\mathbf{s}^{(j)}\}`$ is a d-stationary point of problem [Eq. 18](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E18 "In 3 ‣ Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

###### Proof 4.9.

If there is $`\overline{j}`$ such that ([51](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E51 "Equation 51 ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) holds, then $`\mathbf{s}^{{k - 1},\overline{j}} = \mathbf{s}^{{k - 1},{\overline{j} - 1}}`$, i.e., $`\mathbf{s}^{(\overline{j})} = \mathbf{s}^{({\overline{j} - 1})}`$. This, combined with ([52](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E52 "Equation 52 ‣ Theorem 4.5. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) of Theorem [4.5](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Thmtheorem5 "Theorem 4.5. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), yields that $`{\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(\overline{j})} = \mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(\overline{j})} = \mathbf{s}^{(\overline{j})} = \mathbf{s}^{({\overline{j} - 1})}}.`$ Thus by [Eq. 27](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E27 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")-[Eq. 29](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E29 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") in [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), we have for any $`\lambda > 0`$ and any $`d_{\mathbf{z}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{N_{\mathbf{w}} + N_{\mathbf{a}}}`$, $`d_{\mathbf{h}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{rT}`$, $`d_{\mathbf{u}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{rT}`$,

```math
{{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{(\overline{j})},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} \leq {\mathcal{L}\left( {\mathbf{s}^{(\overline{j})} + {\lambda{(d_{\mathbf{z}},0,0)}}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma \right)}},
```

```math
{{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{(\overline{j})},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} \leq {\mathcal{L}\left( {\mathbf{s}^{(\overline{j})} + {\lambda{(0,d_{\mathbf{h}},0)}}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma \right)}},
```

```math
{{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{(\overline{j})},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} \leq {\mathcal{L}\left( {\mathbf{s}^{(\overline{j})} + {\lambda{(0,0,d_{\mathbf{u}})}}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma \right)}}.
```

By Lemma [4.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Thmtheorem2 "Lemma 4.2. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and the definition of the directional derivative, we get for any $`d_{\mathbf{z}}`$, $`d_{\mathbf{h}}`$, $`d_{\mathbf{u}}`$,

$`{{{\mathcal{L}'{(\mathbf{s}^{(\overline{j})},\xi,\zeta,\gamma;{(d_{\mathbf{z}},0,0)})}} \geq 0},{{\mathcal{L}'{(\mathbf{s}^{(\overline{j})},\xi,\zeta,\gamma;{(0,d_{\mathbf{h}},0)})}} \geq 0}},`$$`{{\mathcal{L}'{(\mathbf{s}^{(\overline{j})},\xi,\zeta,\gamma;{(0,0,d_{\mathbf{u}})})}} \geq 0}.`$

The above inequalities, along with [Lemma 4.7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Thmtheorem7 "Lemma 4.7. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), yields that $`{\mathcal{L}'{(\mathbf{s}^{(\overline{j})},\xi,\zeta,\gamma;d)}} \geq 0`$ for any $`{d \in {\mathbb{R}}^{N_{\mathbf{w}} + N_{\mathbf{a}} + {2rT}}}.`$ Hence, $`\mathbf{s}^{(\overline{j})}`$ is a d-stationary point of problem [Eq. 18](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E18 "In 3 ‣ Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

If there is no $`\overline{j}`$ such that ([51](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E51 "Equation 51 ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) holds, then [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") generates an infinite sequence $`\{\mathbf{s}^{(j)}\}`$. By [Eq. 29](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E29 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), we have

$`{{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} \leq {{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} + {\frac{\mu}{2}{\|{\mathbf{u}^{(j)} - \mathbf{u}^{({j - 1})}}\|}^{2}}} \leq {\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}}.`$

Letting $`j\rightarrow\infty`$ in the above inequalities and using [Theorem 4.5](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Thmtheorem5 "Theorem 4.5. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") (i), we have

$`{{\lim\limits_{j\rightarrow\infty}{\|{\mathbf{u}^{(j)} - \mathbf{u}^{({j - 1})}}\|}} = 0}.`$

By [Theorem 4.5](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Thmtheorem5 "Theorem 4.5. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") (ii), let $`\{\mathbf{s}_{z}^{(j_{i})}\}`$, $`\{\mathbf{s}_{h}^{(j_{i})}\}`$ and $`\{\mathbf{s}^{(j_{i})}\}`$ be any convergent subsequences with limit $`\overline{\mathbf{s}}`$. Furthermore, by [Eq. 27](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E27 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")-[Eq. 29](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E29 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") in [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), we have for any $`\lambda > 0`$ and any $`d_{\mathbf{z}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{N_{\mathbf{w}} + N_{\mathbf{a}}}`$, $`d_{\mathbf{h}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{rT}`$, $`d_{\mathbf{u}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{rT}`$,

```math
{{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(j_{i})},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} \leq {\mathcal{L}\left( {\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(j_{i})} + {\lambda{(d_{\mathbf{z}},0,0)}}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma \right)}},
```

```math
{{\mathcal{L}\left( \mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(j_{i})},\xi,\zeta,\gamma \right)} \leq {\mathcal{L}\left( {\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(j_{i})} + {\lambda{(0,d_{\mathbf{h}},0)}}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma \right)}},
```

```math
{{\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s}^{(j_{i})},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} \leq {{\mathcal{L}\left( {\mathbf{s}^{(j_{i})} + {\lambda{(0,0,d_{\mathbf{u}})}}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma \right)} + {\frac{\mu}{2}{\|{{\mathbf{u}^{(j_{i})} + {\lambda d_{\mathbf{u}}}} - \mathbf{u}^{({j_{i} - 1})}}\|}^{2}}}}.
```

As $`i\rightarrow\infty`$, the above equality and inequalities imply that for any $`\lambda > 0`$ and any $`d_{\mathbf{z}}`$, $`d_{\mathbf{h}}`$, $`d_{\mathbf{u}}`$,

```math
\begin{aligned}
{{{\mathcal{L}{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} \leq {\mathcal{L}\left( {\overline{\mathbf{s}} + {\lambda{(d_{\mathbf{z}},0,0)}}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma \right)}},} & {{{\mathcal{L}{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} \leq {\mathcal{L}\left( {\overline{\mathbf{s}} + {\lambda{(0,d_{\mathbf{h}},0)}}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma \right)}},} \\
{\mathcal{L}{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)} \leq \mathcal{L}\left( \overline{\mathbf{s}} + \lambda{(0,0,} \right.} & {\left. {d_{\mathbf{u}})},\xi,\zeta,\gamma \right) + \frac{\mu}{2}\lambda^{2} \parallel d_{\mathbf{u}} \parallel^{2}.}
\end{aligned}
```

By [Lemma 4.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Thmtheorem2 "Lemma 4.2. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and the definition of directional derivative, it follows that

$`{{{\mathcal{L}'{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma;{(d_{\mathbf{z}},0,0)})}} \geq 0},{{{\mathcal{L}'{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma;{(0,d_{\mathbf{h}},0)})}} \geq 0},{{\mathcal{L}'{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma;{(0,0,d_{\mathbf{u}})})}} \geq 0}}},`$

for any $`d_{\mathbf{z}}`$, $`d_{\mathbf{h}}`$ and $`d_{\mathbf{u}}`$. The above inequalities, along with [Lemma 4.7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Thmtheorem7 "Lemma 4.7. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), yield that $`\overline{\mathbf{s}}`$ is a d-stationary point of problem [Eq. 18](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E18 "In 3 ‣ Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

### 4.2 Convergence analysis of [Algorithm 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg1 "In 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")

By [Theorem 4.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Thmtheorem3 "Theorem 4.3. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), the ALM in [Algorithm 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg1 "In 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") is well-defined, since Step 1 can always be fulfilled in finite steps by the BCD method in [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

It is well known that the classical ALM may converge to an infeasible point. In contrast, the following theorem guarantees that any accumulation point of the ALM in Algorithm [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") is a feasible point. The delicate strategy for updating the penalty parameter $`\gamma_{k}`$ in Step 3 of Algorithm [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") plays an important role in the proof of the theorem.

###### Theorem 4.10.

Let $`\left\{ \mathbf{s}^{k} \right\}`$ be the sequence generated by [Algorithm 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg1 "In 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). Then the following statements hold.

1.  (i)
    $`{\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\| {\mathbf{u}^{k} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h}^{k})}\mathbf{w}^{k}}} \right\|} = 0`$ and $`{\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\| {\mathbf{h}^{k} - {(\mathbf{u}^{k})}_{+}} \right\|} = 0`$.
2.  (ii)
    There exists at least one accumulation point of $`\{\mathbf{s}^{k}\}`$, and any accumulation point is a feasible point of ([7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "Equation 7 ‣ 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")).

###### Proof 4.11.

[(i)](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.I2.i1 "Item (i) ‣ Theorem 4.10. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") Let the index set

$`{\mathcal{K}:=\left\{ k:{\gamma_{k} = {\max{\{{\gamma_{k - 1}/\eta_{2}},{\|\xi^{k}\|}^{1 + \eta_{3}},{\|\zeta^{k}\|}^{1 + \eta_{3}}\}}}} \right\}}.`$

If $`\mathcal{K}`$ is a finite set, then there exists $`K \in {\mathbb{N}}_{+}`$, such that for all $`k > K`$,

$`\max\left\{ {\|{\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s}^{k})}}\|},{\|{\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s}^{k})}}\|} \right\}`$$`\leq`$$`\eta_{1}{\max\left\{ {\|{\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s}^{k - 1})}}\|},{\|{\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s}^{k - 1})}}\|} \right\}}`$$`\leq`$$`{\eta_{1}^{k - K}{\max\left\{ {\|{\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s}^{K})}}\|},{\|{\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s}^{K})}}\|} \right\}}}.`$

Since $`\eta_{1} \in {(0,1)}`$, we get $`\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}`$ $`{{\max\left\{ {\|{\mathbf{u}^{k} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h}^{k})}\mathbf{w}^{k}}}\|},{\|{\mathbf{h}^{k} - {(\mathbf{u}^{k})}_{+}}\|} \right\}} = 0}.`$ The statement (i) can thus be proved for this case.

Otherwise, $`\mathcal{K}`$ is an infinite set. Then for those $`{k - 1} \in \mathcal{K}`$,

```math
{{{\max\left\{ \frac{\left\| \xi^{k - 1} \right\|}{\gamma_{k - 1}},\frac{\left\| \zeta^{k - 1} \right\|}{\gamma_{k - 1}} \right\}} \leq \left( \gamma_{k - 1} \right)^{\frac{- \eta_{3}}{1 + \eta_{3}}}},{{\max\left\{ \frac{\left\| \xi^{k - 1} \right\|^{2}}{\gamma_{k - 1}},\frac{\left\| \zeta^{k - 1} \right\|^{2}}{\gamma_{k - 1}} \right\}} \leq \left( \gamma_{k - 1} \right)^{\frac{1 - \eta_{3}}{1 + \eta_{3}}}}}.
```

The above inequalities, together with $`\eta_{3} > 1`$ yields that

```math
{{\lim\limits_{{k\rightarrow\infty},{{k - 1} \in \mathcal{K}}}{\max\left\{ \frac{\left\| \xi^{k - 1} \right\|}{\gamma_{k - 1}},\frac{\left\| \zeta^{k - 1} \right\|}{\gamma_{k - 1}},\frac{\left\| \xi^{k - 1} \right\|^{2}}{\gamma_{k - 1}},\frac{\left\| \zeta^{k - 1} \right\|^{2}}{\gamma_{k - 1}} \right\}}} = 0}.
```

Recalling ([3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.Ex11 "3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")), and employing condition [Eq. 102](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#A1.E102 "In Proof A.5. ‣ A.5 Proof of Lemma 4.1 ‣ Appendix A Proofs of the lemmas ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and Step 1 of [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), we have

```math
\begin{aligned}
{0 \leq} & {\left\| {{\mathbf{u}^{k} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h}^{k})}\mathbf{w}^{k}}} + \frac{\xi^{k - 1}}{\gamma_{k - 1}}} \right\|^{2} + \left\| {{\mathbf{h}^{k} - {(\mathbf{u}^{k})}_{+}} + \frac{\zeta^{k - 1}}{\gamma_{k - 1}}} \right\|^{2}} \\
 \leq & {{{\frac{2}{\gamma_{k - 1}}\left( {\Gamma - {\mathcal{R}{(\mathbf{s}^{k})}}} \right)} + \left( \frac{\|\xi^{k - 1}\|}{\gamma_{k - 1}} \right)^{2} + \left( \frac{\|\zeta^{k - 1}\|}{\gamma_{k - 1}} \right)^{2}}.}
\end{aligned}
```

Then by [Eq. 57](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E57 "In Proof 4.11. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and the lower boundedness of $`\left\{ {\mathcal{R}{(\mathbf{s}^{k})}} \right\}`$, we have

$`{{{\lim\limits_{{k\rightarrow\infty},{{k - 1} \in \mathcal{K}}}{\|{\mathbf{u}^{k} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h}^{k})}\mathbf{w}^{k}}}\|}} = {0\quad\text{and}}}\mspace{21mu}{{\lim\limits_{{k\rightarrow\infty},{{k - 1} \in \mathcal{K}}}{\|{\mathbf{h}^{k} - {(\mathbf{u}^{k})}_{+}}\|}} = 0}}.`$

To extend the results in [Eq. 59](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E59 "In Proof 4.11. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") to any $`k > K`$, let $`l_{k}`$ denote the largest element in $`\mathcal{K}`$ satisfying $`l_{k} < k`$. If $`l_{k} = {k - 1}`$, the limitations are the same as [Eq. 59](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E59 "In Proof 4.11. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). If $`l_{k} < {k - 1}`$, let us define an index set $`\mathcal{I}_{k}:=\left\{ i:{l_{k} < i < k} \right\}`$. The updating rule for the penalty parameter, as stated in [Eq. 22](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E22 "In 6 ‣ Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), implies that $`\gamma_{i} = \gamma_{l_{k}}`$. This, combined with the updating rules for the Lagrangian multipliers, yields that for all $`i \in \mathcal{I}_{k}`$, the following holds:

$`\frac{\|\xi^{i}\|}{\gamma_{i}}`$$`{= \frac{\|\xi^{i}\|}{\gamma_{i - 1}} \leq {\frac{\|\xi^{i - 1}\|}{\gamma_{i - 1}} + \left\| {\mathbf{u}^{i} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h}^{i})}\mathbf{w}^{i}}} \right\|}},`$$`\frac{\|\zeta^{i}\|}{\gamma_{i}}`$$`{= \frac{\|\zeta^{i}\|}{\gamma_{i - 1}} \leq {\frac{\|\zeta^{i - 1}\|}{\gamma_{i - 1}} + \left\| {\mathbf{h}^{i} - {(\mathbf{u}^{i})}_{+}} \right\|}}.`$

Summing up inequalities [Eq. 60](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E60 "In Proof 4.11. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and [Eq. 61](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E61 "In Proof 4.11. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") for every $`i \in \mathcal{I}_{k}`$, we have

$`\frac{\|\xi^{k - 1}\|}{\gamma_{k - 1}}`$$`{\leq {\frac{\|\xi^{l_{k}}\|}{\gamma_{l_{k}}} + {\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{k - l_{k} - 1}\left\| {\mathbf{u}^{k - i} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h}^{k - i})}\mathbf{w}^{k - i}}} \right\|}}},`$$`\frac{\|\zeta^{k - 1}\|}{\gamma_{k - 1}}`$$`{\leq {\frac{\|\zeta^{l_{k}}\|}{\gamma_{l_{k}}} + {\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{k - l_{k} - 1}\left\| {\mathbf{h}^{k - i} - {(\mathbf{u}^{k - i})}_{+}} \right\|}}}.`$

By the updating rule of $`\gamma_{k}`$ in ([21](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E21 "Equation 21In Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")), [Eq. 62](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E62 "In Proof 4.11. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and [Eq. 63](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E63 "In Proof 4.11. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), we obtain

$`{\frac{\left\| \xi^{k - 1} \right\|}{\gamma_{k - 1}} \leq {\frac{\left\| \xi^{l_{k}} \right\|}{\gamma_{l_{k}}} + {\frac{\eta_{1}}{1 - \eta_{1}}{\max\left\{ \left\| {\mathbf{u}^{l_{k} + 1} - {\Psi\left( \mathbf{h}^{l_{k} + 1} \right)\mathbf{w}^{l_{k} + 1}}} \right\|,\left\| {\mathbf{h}^{l_{k} + 1} - \left( \mathbf{u}^{l_{k} + 1} \right)_{+}} \right\| \right\}}}}},`$$`{\frac{\left\| \zeta^{k - 1} \right\|}{\gamma_{k - 1}} \leq {\frac{\left\| \zeta^{l_{k}} \right\|}{\gamma_{l_{k}}} + {\frac{\eta_{1}}{1 - \eta_{1}}{\max\left\{ \left\| {\mathbf{u}^{l_{k} + 1} - {\Psi\left( \mathbf{h}^{l_{k} + 1} \right)\mathbf{w}^{l_{k} + 1}}} \right\|,\left\| {\mathbf{h}^{l_{k} + 1} - \left( \mathbf{u}^{l_{k} + 1} \right)_{+}} \right\| \right\}}}}}.`$

This, together with [Eq. 57](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E57 "In Proof 4.11. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), [Eq. 59](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E59 "In Proof 4.11. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and $`\eta_{1} \in {(0,1)}`$, yields that

$`{{{\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\left\| \xi^{k - 1} \right\|}{\gamma_{k - 1}}} = 0},{{\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\left\| \zeta^{k - 1} \right\|}{\gamma_{k - 1}}} = 0}}.`$

By the inequality [Eq. 58](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E58 "In Proof 4.11. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and nondecreasing sequence $`\{\gamma_{k}\}`$, we conclude that

$`{{{\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}{\|{\mathbf{u}^{k} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h}^{k})}\mathbf{w}^{k}}}\|}} = 0},{{\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}{\|{\mathbf{h}^{k} - {(\mathbf{u}^{k})}_{+}}\|}} = 0}},`$

using the same manner for showing [Eq. 59](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E59 "In Proof 4.11. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

[(ii)](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.I2.i2 "Item (ii) ‣ Theorem 4.10. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") When $`\mathcal{K}`$ is finite, there exists a constant $`K`$ such that $`\gamma_{k - 1} = \gamma_{K}`$ for those $`k > K`$. Then, we turn to consider the boundedness of $`\{\xi^{k - 1}\}`$ and $`\{\zeta^{k - 1}\}`$. Summing up [Eq. 20](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E20 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") for those $`k > K`$, and using ([21](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E21 "Equation 21In Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")), we find

$`\max{\{{\{ \parallel \xi^{k - 1} \parallel , \parallel \zeta^{k - 1} \parallel \}}}`$$`{\leq {{\max{\{{\|\xi^{K}\|},{\|\zeta^{K}\|}\}}} + {\frac{\eta_{1}\gamma_{K}}{1 - \eta_{1}}{\max\left\{ \left\| {\mathbf{u}^{K} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h}^{K})}\mathbf{w}^{K}}} \right\|,\left\| {\mathbf{h}^{K} - {(\mathbf{u}^{K})}_{+}} \right\| \right\}}}}}.`$

From the above, the boundedness of $`\{\xi^{k - 1}\}`$ and $`\{\zeta^{k - 1}\}`$ are thus proved. Together with $`\gamma_{k - 1} = \gamma_{K}`$ for those $`k > K`$, we can further deduce that $`{\|\xi^{k - 1}\|}^{2}/\gamma_{k - 1}`$ and $`{\|\zeta^{k - 1}\|}^{2}/\gamma_{k - 1}`$ are bounded for those $`k \in {\mathbb{N}}_{+}`$.

When the set $`\mathcal{K}`$ is infinite, by [Eq. 57](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E57 "In Proof 4.11. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") we know that $`{\|\xi^{k - 1}\|}^{2}/\gamma_{k - 1}`$ and $`{\|\zeta^{k - 1}\|}^{2}/\gamma_{k - 1}`$ are bounded for $`{k - 1} \in \mathcal{K}`$. Therefore, no matter $`\mathcal{K}`$ is finite or infinite, $`{\|\xi^{k - 1}\|}^{2}/\gamma_{k - 1}`$ and $`{\|\zeta^{k - 1}\|}^{2}/\gamma_{k - 1}`$ are bounded for $`{k - 1} \in \mathcal{K}`$.

Moreover, we can deduce the following inequality according to the expression of $`\mathcal{L}_{k - 1}`$, condition [Eq. 102](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#A1.E102 "In Proof A.5. ‣ A.5 Proof of Lemma 4.1 ‣ Appendix A Proofs of the lemmas ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), and $`\mathbf{s}^{k} = \mathbf{s}^{{k - 1},j}`$:

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{\mathcal{R}{(\mathbf{s}^{k})}} + {\frac{\gamma_{k - 1}}{2}\left\| {{\mathbf{u}^{k} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h}^{k})}\mathbf{w}^{k}}} + \frac{\xi^{k - 1}}{\gamma_{k - 1}}} \right\|^{2}} + {\frac{\gamma_{k - 1}}{2}\left\| {{\mathbf{h}^{k} - {(\mathbf{u}^{k})}_{+}} + \frac{\zeta^{k - 1}}{\gamma_{k - 1}}} \right\|^{2}}} \\
 \leq & {{\Gamma + \frac{{\|\xi^{k - 1}\|}^{2}}{2\gamma_{k - 1}} + \frac{{\|\zeta^{k - 1}\|}^{2}}{2\gamma_{k - 1}}}.}
\end{array}
```

The above inequality, along with the boundedness of $`\left\{ {{\|\xi^{k - 1}\|}^{2}/\gamma_{k - 1}} \right\}_{{k - 1} \in \mathcal{K}}`$ and $`\left\{ {{\|\zeta^{k - 1}\|}^{2}/\gamma_{k - 1}} \right\}_{{k - 1} \in \mathcal{K}}`$, yields the boundedness of $`{\{\mathbf{s}^{k}\}}_{{k - 1} \in \mathcal{K}}`$ by the same manner in [Lemma 3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") [(ii)](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.I1.i2 "Item (ii) ‣ Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). Hence there exists at least one accumulation point of $`\{\mathbf{s}^{k}\}`$.

Any accumulation point is a feasible point of [Eq. 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), which can be derived immediately by (i), because of the continuity of the functions in the constraints of [Eq. 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

Below we show the main convergence result of the ALM.

###### Theorem 4.12.

Every accumulation point of $`\{\mathbf{s}^{k}\}`$ generated by [Algorithm 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg1 "In 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") is a KKT point of problem [Eq. 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

###### Proof 4.13.

Let $`\{\mathbf{s}^{k_{i}}\}`$ be a subsequence of $`\{\mathbf{s}^{k}\}`$ converging to $`\overline{\mathbf{s}}`$. Then $`\overline{s} \in \mathcal{F}`$ by [Theorem 4.10](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Thmtheorem10 "Theorem 4.10. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). We claim that

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {\partial{\mathcal{L}\left( \mathbf{s}^{k_{i}},\xi^{k_{i} - 1},\zeta^{k_{i} - 1},\gamma_{k_{i} - 1} \right)}} \\
 = & {{{\nabla\mathcal{R}}{(\mathbf{s}^{k_{i}})}} + {\nabla_{\mathbf{s}}\left( {{\langle\xi^{k_{i} - 1},{\mathbf{u}^{k_{i}} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h}^{k_{i}})}\mathbf{w}^{k_{i}}}}\rangle} + {\frac{\gamma_{k_{i} - 1}}{2}\left\| {\mathbf{u}^{k_{i}} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h}^{k_{i}})}\mathbf{w}^{k_{i}}}} \right\|^{2}}} \right)}} \\
 & {+ {\partial_{\mathbf{s}}\left( {{\langle\zeta^{k_{i} - 1},{\mathbf{h}^{k_{i}} - {(\mathbf{u}^{k_{i}})}_{+}}\rangle} + {\frac{\gamma_{k_{i} - 1}}{2}\left\| {\mathbf{h}^{k_{i}} - {(\mathbf{u}^{k_{i}})}_{+}} \right\|^{2}}} \right)}} \\
 = & {{{{\nabla\mathcal{R}}{(\mathbf{s}^{k_{i}})}} + {J\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s}^{k_{i}})}^{\top}\xi^{k_{i}}} + {\partial\left( {{(\zeta^{k_{i}})}^{\top}\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s}^{k_{i}})}} \right)}},}
\end{array}
```

where $`\mathcal{C}_{1}`$ and $`\mathcal{C}_{2}`$ are defined in [Eq. 5](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E5 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

First, by employing [Eq. 20](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E20 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and by direct computation, we have

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {\nabla_{\mathbf{s}}\left( {{\langle\xi^{k_{i} - 1},{\mathbf{u}^{k_{i}} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h}^{k_{i}})}\mathbf{w}^{k_{i}}}}\rangle} + {\frac{\gamma_{k_{i} - 1}}{2}\left\| {\mathbf{u}^{k_{i}} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h}^{k_{i}})}\mathbf{w}^{k_{i}}}} \right\|^{2}}} \right)} \\
 = & {{{J\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s}^{k_{i}})}^{\top}\left( {\xi^{k_{i} - 1} + {\gamma_{k_{i} - 1}{({\mathbf{u}^{k_{i}} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h}^{k_{i}})}\mathbf{w}^{k_{i}}}})}}} \right)} = {J\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s}^{k_{i}})}^{\top}\xi^{k_{i}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Then, it remains to verify that

$`{{\partial_{\mathbf{s}}{({{\langle\zeta^{k_{i} - 1},{\mathbf{h}^{k_{i}} - {(\mathbf{u}^{k_{i}})}_{+}}\rangle} + {\frac{\gamma_{k_{i} - 1}}{2}{\|{\mathbf{h}^{k_{i}} - {(\mathbf{u}^{k_{i}})}_{+}}\|}^{2}}})}} = {\partial\left( {{(\zeta^{k_{i}})}^{\top}\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s}^{k_{i}})}} \right)}}.`$

To verify [Eq. 69](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E69 "In Proof 4.13. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), it can be divided into the subdifferential associated with $`\mathbf{h}`$ and $`\mathbf{u}`$. We first prove that [Eq. 69](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E69 "In Proof 4.13. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") is satisfied associated with $`\mathbf{h}`$. By simple computation,

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {\nabla_{\mathbf{h}}\left( {{\langle\zeta^{k_{i} - 1},{\mathbf{h}^{k_{i}} - {(\mathbf{u}^{k_{i}})}_{+}}\rangle} + {\frac{\gamma_{k_{i} - 1}}{2}\left\| {\mathbf{h}^{k_{i}} - {(\mathbf{u}^{k_{i}})}_{+}} \right\|^{2}}} \right)} \\
 = & {J_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{z}^{k_{i}},\mathbf{h}^{k_{i}},\mathbf{u}^{k_{i}})}^{\top}\left( {\zeta^{k_{i} - 1} + {\gamma_{k_{i} - 1}{({\mathbf{h}^{k_{i}} - {(\mathbf{u}^{k_{i}})}_{+}})}}} \right)} \\
 = & {{{J_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{z}^{k_{i}},\mathbf{h}^{k_{i}},\mathbf{u}^{k_{i}})}^{\top}\zeta^{k_{i}}} = {\nabla_{\mathbf{h}}\left( {\langle\zeta^{k_{i}},{\mathbf{h}^{k_{i}} - {(\mathbf{u}^{k_{i}})}_{+}}\rangle} \right)}}.}
\end{array}
```

Then we prove that [Eq. 69](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E69 "In Proof 4.13. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") is satisfied associated with $`\mathbf{u}`$, which can be replaced by proving $`rT`$ one dimensional equations with the similar structure as follows:

$`{{\partial_{\mathbf{u}_{j}}\left( {{\zeta_{j}^{k_{i} - 1}{({\mathbf{h}_{j}^{k_{i}} - {(\mathbf{u}_{j}^{k_{i}})}_{+}})}} + {\frac{\gamma_{k_{i} - 1}}{2}{({\mathbf{h}_{j}^{k_{i}} - {(\mathbf{u}_{j}^{k_{i}})}_{+}})}^{2}}} \right)} = {\partial_{\mathbf{u}_{j}}\left( {\zeta_{j}^{k_{i}}{({\mathbf{h}_{j}^{k_{i}} - {(\mathbf{u}_{j}^{k_{i}})}_{+}})}} \right)}},`$

where $`j = {1,2,\ldots,{rT}}`$.
