When $`\mathbf{u}_{j}^{k_{i}} \neq 0`$, equation [Eq. 71](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E71 "In Proof 4.13. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") can be easily deduced by the same proof method as in LABEL:subdiff*q_h. When $`\mathbf{u}*{j}^{k\_{i}} = 0`$, the validity of ([71](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E71 "Equation 71 ‣ Proof 4.13. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) can be proved as follows:

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {\partial_{\mathbf{u}_{j}}\left( {{\zeta_{j}^{k_{i} - 1}{({\mathbf{h}_{j}^{k_{i}} - {(\mathbf{u}_{j}^{k_{i}})}_{+}})}} + {\frac{\gamma_{k_{i} - 1}}{2}{({\mathbf{h}_{j}^{k_{i}} - {(\mathbf{u}_{j}^{k_{i}})}_{+}})}^{2}}} \right)} \\
 = & \begin{cases}
{{\{ 0,{{- \zeta_{j}^{k_{i} - 1}} - {\gamma_{k_{i} - 1}{({\mathbf{h}_{j}^{k_{i}} - \mathbf{u}_{j}^{k_{i}}})}}}\}},} & {{{{\text{if}\gamma_{k_{i} - 1}\mathbf{h}_{j}^{k_{i}}} + \zeta_{j}^{k_{i} - 1}} \geq 0},} \\
{\left\lbrack 0,{{- \zeta_{j}^{k_{i} - 1}} - {\gamma_{k_{i} - 1}{({\mathbf{h}_{j}^{k_{i}} - \mathbf{u}_{j}^{k_{i}}})}}} \right\rbrack,} & {{{{\text{if}\gamma_{k_{i} - 1}\mathbf{h}_{j}^{k_{i}}} + \zeta_{j}^{k_{i} - 1}} < 0},}
\end{cases} \\
 = & \begin{cases}
{{\{ 0,{- \zeta_{j}^{k_{i}}}\}},} & {{{\text{if}\zeta_{j}^{k_{i}}} \geq 0},} \\
{\left\lbrack 0,{- \zeta_{j}^{k_{i}}} \right\rbrack,} & {{{\text{if}\zeta_{j}^{k_{i}}} < 0},}
\end{cases} \\
 = & {{\partial_{\mathbf{u}_{j}}\left( {\zeta_{j}^{k_{i}}{({\mathbf{h}_{j}^{k_{i}} - {(\mathbf{u}_{j}^{k_{i}})}_{+}})}} \right)}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining LABEL:KKT_grad_smooth and [Eq. 69](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E69 "In Proof 4.13. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") yields the validity of LABEL:eq:AL_update.

Up to now, we have verified that equation LABEL:eq:AL*update holds. Thus, there exists a sequence $`\{\varsigma^{k*{i}}\}`$ satisfying $`{\|\varsigma^{k*{i}}\|} \leq \epsilon^{k*{i}}`$ such that

$`{\varsigma^{k_{i}} \in {{{\nabla\mathcal{R}}{(\mathbf{s}^{k_{i}})}} + {J\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s}^{k_{i}})}^{\top}\xi^{k_{i}}} + {\partial\left( {{(\zeta^{k_{i}})}^{\top}\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s}^{k_{i}})}} \right)}}}.`$

However, the boundedness of $`\{\xi^{k_{i}}\}`$ and $`\{\zeta^{k_{i}}\}`$ in [Eq. 73](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E73 "In Proof 4.13. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") are still not sure. Define $`\varrho^{i}`$ $`= {\max{\{{\|\xi^{k_{i}}\|}_{\infty},{\|\zeta^{k_{i}}\|}_{\infty}\}}}`$ and assume that $`\{\varrho^{i}\}`$ is unbounded. It is trivial to have bounded sequences $`\{{\xi^{k_{i}}/\varrho^{i}}\}`$ and $`\{{\zeta^{k_{i}}/\varrho^{i}}\}`$ according to the definition of $`\varrho^{i}`$. Without loss of generality, we assume $`\{{\xi^{k_{i}}/\varrho^{i}}\}`$ $`\rightarrow`$ $`\overline{\xi}`$ and $`{\{{\zeta^{k_{i}}/\varrho^{i}}\}}\rightarrow\overline{\zeta}`$ as $`k\rightarrow\infty`$ and thus have

$`{{\max{\{{\|\overline{\xi}\|}_{\infty},{\|\overline{\zeta}\|}_{\infty}\}}} = 1}.`$

Dividing by $`\varrho^{i}`$ on both sides of [Eq. 73](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E73 "In Proof 4.13. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and taking $`i\rightarrow\infty`$, and using the facts that the limiting subdifferential is outer semicontinuous \[[26](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib26), Proposition 8.7\], and $`\varsigma^{k_{i}}\rightarrow 0`$ as $`i\rightarrow\infty`$, we derive that

$`{0 \in {{J\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\overline{\mathbf{s}})}^{\top}\overline{\xi}} + {\partial\left( {{\overline{\zeta}}^{\top}\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\overline{\mathbf{s}})}} \right)}}}.`$

Combining [Eq. 75](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E75 "In Proof 4.13. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and [Lemma 2.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma 2.1. ‣ 2.2 Optimality conditions ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") yields that $`\overline{\xi} = 0`$ and $`\overline{\zeta} = 0`$, which contradicts [Eq. 74](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E74 "In Proof 4.13. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). Therefore, $`\{\xi^{k_{i}}\}`$ and $`\{\zeta^{k_{i}}\}`$ are bounded. Without loss of generality, we assume $`{\{\xi^{k_{i}}\}}\rightarrow\overline{\xi}`$ and $`{\{\zeta^{k_{i}}\}}\rightarrow\overline{\zeta}`$ as $`i\rightarrow\infty`$. Letting $`i\rightarrow\infty`$ in [Eq. 73](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E73 "In Proof 4.13. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), we obtain

```math
{0 \in {{{\nabla\mathcal{R}}{(\overline{\mathbf{s}})}} + {J\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\overline{\mathbf{s}})}^{\top}\overline{\xi}} + {\partial\left( {{\overline{\zeta}}^{\top}\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\overline{\mathbf{s}})}} \right)}}}.
```

Therefore, $`\overline{\mathbf{s}}`$ is a KKT point of problem [Eq. 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

### 4.3 Extensions to other activation functions

Now we discuss the possible extensions of our methods, algorithms and theoretical analysis, using other activation functions rather than the ReLU.

First, we claim that the activation functions are required to be locally Lipschitz continuous, because the locally Lipschitz continuity of the ReLU function is used in $`L_{2}{(\xi,\zeta,\gamma,\hat{r})}`$ of Lemma [3.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.Thmtheorem2 "Lemma 3.2. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") that depends on the Lipschitz constant of the ReLU function on a compact set. Then we find that in the analysis above only the following two places make use of the special piecewise linear structure of the ReLU function:

- P1.
  Explicit formula for $`\mathbf{u}^{{k - 1},j}`$ in ([29](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E29 "Equation 29In Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) of the BCD method in Algorithm [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").
- P2.
  Equations (LABEL:subdiff_q_u_left) for proving ([71](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E71 "Equation 71 ‣ Proof 4.13. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) in the proof of Theorem [4.12](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Thmtheorem12 "Theorem 4.12. ‣ 4.2 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

For P1, even if the activation function in ([2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E2 "Equation 2 ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) is replaced by others, the objective function in problem ([29](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E29 "Equation 29In Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) can still be separated into $`rT`$ one-dimensional functions, which is obtained by substituting the ReLU function $`{(u)}_{+}`$ in ([32](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E32 "Equation 32 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) by a more general activation function. For P2, if an arbitrary smooth activation function is considered, then (4.29) holds obviously because the limiting subdifferential reduces to the gradient. Below we illustrate in detail the leaky ReLU and the ELU activation functions as examples for extensions. It is clear that the expression of $`L_{2}{(\xi,\zeta,\gamma,\hat{r})}`$ in [Lemma 3.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.Thmtheorem2 "Lemma 3.2. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") remains unchanged for the two activation functions because they all have Lipschitz constant 1, the same as that of the ReLU.

#### Extension to the leaky ReLU

Let us replace the ReLU activation function $`{\sigma{(u)}} = {(u)}_{+}`$ with the leaky ReLU activation function defined by

$`{{\sigma_{lRe}{(u)}}:={\max{\{ u,{\varpi u}\}}}},`$

where $`\varpi \in {(0,1)}`$ is a fixed parameter. The leaky ReLU activation function has been widely used in recent years. With regard to P1, by direct computation, a closed-form global solution of

```math
{{\min\limits_{u \in {\mathbb{R}}}{\varphi_{lRe}{(u)}}}:={{\frac{\gamma}{2}{({u - \theta_{1}})}^{2}} + {\frac{\gamma}{2}{({\theta_{2} - {\sigma_{lRe}{(u)}}})}^{2}} + {\frac{\mu}{2}{({u - \theta_{3}})}^{2}} + {\lambda_{6}u^{2}}}},
```

can be obtained similarly using the procedures for ReLU in ([33](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E33 "Equation 33 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."))-([39](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E39 "Equation 39 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")), except that the expression $`u^{-}`$ of ([39](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E39 "Equation 39 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) changes to

$`u^{-} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll}
{\frac{{\gamma\theta_{1}} + {\gamma\varpi\theta_{2}} + {\mu\theta_{3}}}{\gamma + {\gamma\varpi^{2}} + {2\lambda_{6}} + \mu},} & {{{{\text{if}\gamma\theta_{1}} + {\mu\theta_{3}}} < 0},} \\
{0,} & {\text{otherwise}.}
\end{array} \right.`$

For P2, (LABEL:subdiff*q_u_left) is modified as follows: when $`\mathbf{u}*{j}^{k\_{i}} = 0`$,

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {\partial_{\mathbf{u}_{j}}\left( {{\zeta_{j}^{k_{i} - 1}{({\mathbf{h}_{j}^{k_{i}} - {\sigma_{lRe}{(\mathbf{u}_{j}^{k_{i}})}}})}} + {\frac{\gamma_{k_{i} - 1}}{2}{({\mathbf{h}_{j}^{k_{i}} - {\sigma_{lRe}{(\mathbf{u}_{j}^{k_{i}})}}})}^{2}}} \right)} \\
 = & \begin{cases}
{{\{{- {\varpi\zeta_{j}^{k_{i}}}},{{- \zeta_{j}^{k_{i} - 1}} - {\gamma_{k_{i} - 1}{({\mathbf{h}_{j}^{k_{i}} - \mathbf{u}_{j}^{k_{i}}})}}}\}},} & {{{{\text{if}\gamma_{k_{i} - 1}\mathbf{h}_{j}^{k_{i}}} + \zeta_{j}^{k_{i} - 1}} \geq 0},} \\
{\left\lbrack {- {\varpi\zeta_{j}^{k_{i}}}},{{- \zeta_{j}^{k_{i} - 1}} - {\gamma_{k_{i} - 1}{({\mathbf{h}_{j}^{k_{i}} - \mathbf{u}_{j}^{k_{i}}})}}} \right\rbrack,} & {{{{\text{if}\gamma_{k_{i} - 1}\mathbf{h}_{j}^{k_{i}}} + \zeta_{j}^{k_{i} - 1}} < 0},}
\end{cases} \\
 = & \begin{cases}
{{\{{- {\varpi\zeta_{j}^{k_{i}}}},{- \zeta_{j}^{k_{i}}}\}},} & {{{\text{if}\zeta_{j}^{k_{i}}} \geq 0},} \\
{\left\lbrack {- {\varpi\zeta_{j}^{k_{i}}}},{- \zeta_{j}^{k_{i}}} \right\rbrack,} & {{{\text{if}\zeta_{j}^{k_{i}}} < 0},}
\end{cases} \\
 = & {{\partial_{\mathbf{u}_{j}}\left( {\zeta_{j}^{k_{i}}{({\mathbf{h}_{j}^{k_{i}} - {\sigma_{lRe}{(\mathbf{u}_{j}^{k_{i}})}}})}} \right)}.}
\end{array}
```

#### Extension to the ELU

Let us replace the ReLU activation function with the convex and smooth activation function ELU defined by

$`{\sigma_{ELU}{(u)}}:=\begin{cases}
u & {{{\text{if~}u} \geq 0},} \\
{e^{u} - 1} & {{{\text{if~}u} < 0}.}
\end{cases}`$

When $`u \geq 0`$, the ELU activation function is the same as the ReLU function. Thus for P1, the solution of ([76](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E76 "Equation 76 ‣ Extension to the leaky ReLU ‣ 4.3 Extensions to other activation functions ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) can be obtained similarly as the ReLU case, except that we do not have the explicit formula of $`u^{-}`$, which is a global solution of

$`{{{\min\limits_{u \in {({- \infty},0\rbrack}}\varphi_{\text{ELU}}}{(u)}} = {{\frac{\gamma}{2}{({u - \theta_{1}})}^{2}} + {\frac{\gamma}{2}{({\theta_{2} - {({e^{u} - 1})}})}^{2}} + {\frac{\mu}{2}{({u - \theta_{3}})}^{2}} + {\lambda_{6}u^{2}}}},`$

due to the presence of the exponential function in the ELU activation function.

Now we illustrate that $`u^{-}`$ can be obtained numerically through solving several one-dimensional minimization problems. First, using the formula of $`\varphi_{ELU}{(u)}`$ and $`{\varphi_{ELU}{(u)}}\rightarrow{+ \infty}`$ as $`u\rightarrow{- \infty}`$, we can easily find a lower bound $`\underset{¯}{u} < 0`$ such that ([81](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E81 "Equation 81 ‣ Extension to the ELU ‣ 4.3 Extensions to other activation functions ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) is equivalent to

```math
{{\min\limits_{u \in {\lbrack\underset{¯}{u},0\rbrack}}\varphi_{ELU}}{(u)}}.
```

The objective function $`\varphi_{\text{ELU}}{(u)}`$ is smooth on $`({- \infty},0\rbrack`$. We thus calculate the second-order derivative of $`\varphi_{\text{ELU}}{(u)}`$ as

$`{{\varphi_{\text{ELU}}^{\operatorname{\prime\prime}}{(u)}} = {{{2\gamma e^{2u}} - {\gamma{({\theta_{2} + 1})}e^{u}}} + \mu + \gamma + {2\lambda_{6}}}}.`$

Letting $`z = e^{u}`$, ([83](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E83 "Equation 83 ‣ Extension to the ELU ‣ 4.3 Extensions to other activation functions ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) can thus be represented as

$`{{\psi_{\text{ELU}}{(z)}}:={{{2\gamma z^{2}} - {\gamma{({\theta_{2} + 1})}z}} + \mu + \gamma + {2\lambda_{6}}}},`$

which is a quadratic function. Hence there are at most two distinct roots of

```math
{{\psi_{ELU}{(z)}} = 0},
```

and consequently at most two distinct roots for $`{\varphi^{\operatorname{\prime\prime}}{(u)}} = 0`$ on $`\lbrack\underset{¯}{u},0\rbrack`$. Hence the convexity and concavity can only be changed at most three times in $`\lbrack\underset{¯}{u},0\rbrack`$. That is, we can divide $`\lbrack\underset{¯}{u},0\rbrack`$ into at most three closed intervals, and in each interval $`\varphi_{ELU}`$ is either convex or concave. We minimize the objective function $`\varphi_{ELU}`$ in each of those intervals that $`\varphi_{ELU}`$ is convex, and obtain a global solution in each interval numerically. Then, we select a point among those solutions, $`0`$, and $`\underset{¯}{u}`$ that has the minimal objective value. This point is a global solution of ([81](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E81 "Equation 81 ‣ Extension to the ELU ‣ 4.3 Extensions to other activation functions ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")).

## 5 Numerical experiments

We employ a real world dataset, Volatility of S$`\&`$P index, and synthetic datasets to evaluate the effectiveness of our reformulation ([7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "Equation 7 ‣ 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) and [Algorithm 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg1 "In 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") with [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). To be specific, we first use RNNs with unknown weighted matrices to model these sequential datasets, and then utilize the ALM with the BCD method to train RNNs. After the training process, we can predict future values of these sequential datasets using the trained RNNs.

The numerical experiments consist of two components. The first part involves assessing whether the outputs generated by the ALM adhere to the constraints in [Eq. 7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "In 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). The second part is to compare the training and forecasting performance of the ALM with state-of-the-art gradient descent-based algorithms (GDs). All the numerical experiments were conducted using Python 3.9.8. For the datasets, Synthetic dataset ($`T = 10`$) and Volatility of S$`\&`$P index, experiments were carried out on a desktop (Windows 10 with 2.90 GHz Inter Core i7-10700 CPU and 32GB RAM). Additionally, experiments for Synthetic dataset ($`T = 500`$) were implemented on a server (2 Intel Xeon Gold 6248R CPUs and 768GB RAM) at the high-performance servers of the Department of Applied Mathematics, the Hong Kong Polytechnic University.

### 5.1 Datasets

The process of generating synthetic datasets is as follows. We randomly generate the weighted matrices $`\hat{A}`$, $`\hat{W}`$, $`\hat{V}`$, $`\hat{b}`$ and $`\hat{c}`$, noise $`{\overset{\sim}{e}}_{t}`$, $`t = {1,2,\ldots,T}`$ and the input data $`X = {(x_{1},\ldots,x_{T})}`$ with some distributions. Then we calculate the output data $`Y = {(y_{1},\ldots,y_{T})}`$ by $`y_{t} = {{({{\hat{A}{({{\hat{W}{({\ldots{({{\hat{V}x_{1}} + \hat{b}})}_{+}\ldots})}} + {\hat{V}x_{t}} + \hat{b}})}_{+}} + \hat{c}})} + {\overset{\sim}{e}}_{t}}`$ for $`t \in {\lbrack T\rbrack}`$. In the numerical experiments, we generate two synthetic datasets with $`T = 10`$ and $`T = 500`$. The detailed information of the two synthetic datasets is listed in [Table 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S5.T1 "In 5.1 Datasets ‣ 5 Numerical experiments ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). Moreover, the ratio of splitting for the training and test sets is about $`9:1`$.

|       |       |       |       |                         |                                          |                                       |
| ----- | ----- | ----- | ----- | ----------------------- | ---------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------- |
| $`T`$ | $`n`$ | $`m`$ | $`r`$ | Distributions           |                                          |                                       |
|       |       |       |       | weight matrices         | the noise                                | the input data                        |
| 10    | 5     | 3     | 4     | $`\mathcal{N}(0,0.8)`$  | $`\mathcal{N}\left( 0,10^{- 3} \right)`$ | $`\mathcal{U}\left( {- 1},1 \right)`$ |
| 500   | 80    | 30    | 100   | $`\mathcal{N}(0,0.05)`$ | $`\mathcal{N}\left( 0,10^{- 5} \right)`$ | $`\mathcal{U}\left( {- 1},1 \right)`$ |

Table 1: Synthetic datasets

The dataset, Volatility of S$`\&`$P index, consists of the monthly realized volatility of the S$`\&`$P index and 11 corresponding exogenous variables from February 1973 to June 2009, totaling 437 time steps, i.e., $`T = 437`$, $`n = 11`$ and $`m = 1`$. The dataset was collected in strict adherence to the guidelines in \[[6](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib6)\] and contains no missing values. In the dataset, the monthly realized volatility of S$`\&`$P index is appointed as the output variable, while 11 exogenous variables are input variables. For training the RNNs, we first standardize the dataset as zero mean and unit variance, and then allocate 90$`\%`$ of the dataset, consisting of 393 time steps, as the training set, while the remaining 44 time steps are the test set. Moreover, we have $`r = 20`$ for the real dataset.

### 5.2 Evaluations

We define $`\text{FeasVio}:={\text{max}{\{{\|{\mathbf{u} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}\mathbf{w}}}\|},{\|{\mathbf{h} - {(\mathbf{u})}_{+}}\|}\}}}`$ to evaluate the feasibility violation for constraints $`\mathbf{u} = {\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}\mathbf{w}}`$ and $`\mathbf{h} = {(\mathbf{u})}_{+}`$. Moreover, the training and test errors are used to evaluate the forecasting accuracy of RNNs in training and test sets denoted as

$`\text{TrainErr}:=\frac{1}{T_{1}}\sum\limits_{t = 1}^{T_{1}} \parallel y_{t} - {(A{(W{(\ldots{(Vx_{1} + b)}_{+}\ldots)} + Vx_{t} + b)}_{+} + c \parallel^{2}}`$$`{\text{TestErr}:={\frac{1}{T_{2}}{\sum\limits_{t = {T_{1} + 1}}^{T_{1} + T_{2}}{\|{y_{t} - {({{A{({{W{({\ldots{({{Vx_{1}} + b})}_{+}\ldots})}} + {Vx_{t}} + b})}_{+}} + c})}}\|}^{2}}}},`$

where $`T_{1}`$ and $`T_{2}`$ are the time length of the training set and test set, and $`A`$, $`W`$, $`V`$, $`b`$ and $`c`$ are the output solutions from ALM.

### 5.3 Investigating the feasibility

In this subsection, we aim to verify the outputs from the ALM satisfying the constraints of ([3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E3 "Equation 3 ‣ 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) through numerical experiments, while we have already proved the feasibility of any accumulation point of a sequence generated by the ALM in section 4. Initial values of weight matrices $`A^{0},`$ $`W^{0},`$ $`V^{0}`$ are randomly generated from the standard Gaussian distribution $`\mathcal{N}(0,0.1)`$. Moreover, the bias $`b^{0}`$ and $`c^{0}`$ are set as $`0`$. For all three datasets, we stop the outer loop (ALM) when it reaches 100 iterations, and the inner loop (BCD method) terminates at 500 iterations. Other parameters are listed in [Table 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S5.T2 "In 5.3 Investigating the feasibility ‣ 5 Numerical experiments ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

|                                 |                           |                                                                                       |
| ------------------------------- | ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Datasets                        | Regularization parameters | Algorithm parameters                                                                  |
| Synthetic dataset ($`T = 10`$)  | $`\tau = 1.2`$            | $`\eta_{1} = 0.99`$, $`\eta_{2} = {5/6}`$, $`\eta_{3} = 0.01`$, $`\eta_{4} = {5/6}`$. |
| Volatility of S$`\&`$P index    | $`\tau = 1`$              |                                                                                       |
| Synthetic dataset ($`T = 500`$) | $`\tau = 500`$            | $`\eta_{1} = 0.90`$, $`\eta_{2} = 0.90`$, $`\eta_{3} = 0.015`$, $`\eta_{4} = 0.8`$.   |

Table 2: Parameters of the ALM: the parameters for the given datasets are set as $`\gamma^{0} = 1`$, $`\xi^{0} = \mathbf{0}`$, $`\zeta^{0} = \mathbf{0}`$, $`\epsilon_{0} = 0.1`$, $`\Gamma = 10^{2}`$, $`\mu = 10^{- 5}`$, $`\lambda_{1} = {{\tau/r}m}`$, $`\lambda_{2} = {\tau/r^{2}}`$, $`\lambda_{3} = {{\tau/r}n}`$, $`\lambda_{4} = {\tau/r}`$, $`\lambda_{5} = {\tau/m}`$, $`\lambda_{6} = 10^{- 8}`$.

From [Fig. 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S5.F1 "In 5.3 Investigating the feasibility ‣ 5 Numerical experiments ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), we observe that the feasibility violation in each dataset is very small at the beginning, which implies that the selected initial point is feasible. As it turns to the first iteration, the feasibility violation goes to a large value. After that, the value goes to exhibit an oscillatory decrease and tends to zero. This indicates that the points generated by the ALM gradually satisfy the constraint conditions as the number of iterations increases.

### 5.4 Comparisons with state-of-the-art GDs

In this subsection, we compare the training and forecasting accuracy of RNNs using different methods. Specifically, we compare our ALM with the state-of-the-art GDs and SGDs with special techniques, i.e., gradient descent (GD), gradient descent with gradient clipping (GDC), gradient descent with Nesterov momentum (GDNM), Mini-batch SGD and Adam.

For the initial values of $`A^{0}`$, $`W^{0}`$, $`V^{0}`$, we use the following initialization strategies: random normal initialization \[[2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib2)\] with zero mean and standard deviations of $`10^{- 3}`$ and $`10^{- 1}`$, He initialization \[[32](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib32)\], Glorot initialization \[[33](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib33)\], and LeCun initialization \[[34](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib34)\]. Notably, the initial values of bias, $`b^{0}`$ and $`c^{0}`$, were both set to $`0`$ according to \[[14](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib14), pp. 305\].

We search the learning rates for GDs and SGDs over $`\{ 10^{- 4}`$, $`10^{- 3}`$, $`10^{- 2}`$, $`10^{- 1}`$, $`1\}`$, as well as the clipping norm of GDC over $`\{ 0.5,1,1.5,2,3,4,5,6\}`$. We employ the leave-P-out cross validation and repeated each method 30 trials with $`P = 1`$ in Synthetic dataset ($`T = 10`$), and $`P = 10`$ in Volatility of S$`\&`$P index and Synthetic dataset ($`T = 500`$). We then select the learning rates and clipping norm with the best test error averaged over 30 trials, which are recorded in Table [5](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#A2.T5 "Table 5 ‣ Appendix B Parameters for numerical experiments in section 5.4 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") of [Appendix B](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#A2 "Appendix B Parameters for numerical experiments in section 5.4 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). The batch size for SGDs is set to 2 for Synthetic dataset ($`T = 10`$), 50 for Volatility of S$`\&`$P index, and 100 for Synthetic dataset ($`T = 500`$). We employ the Keras API \[[10](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib10)\] running on TensorFlow 2 to implement the GDs and SGDs. Additionally, the parameters for the ALM are listed in [Table 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S5.T2 "In 5.3 Investigating the feasibility ‣ 5 Numerical experiments ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).").

To evaluate the performance of different methods under various initialization strategies, we conducted the following experiments: each method was repeated 10 times under each initialization strategy. In each repetition, we recorded the final test error and the training error. We then calculated their means (TrainErr and TestErr) and the corresponding standard deviations, and listed them in [Table 3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S5.T3 "In 5.4 Comparisons with state-of-the-art GDs ‣ 5 Numerical experiments ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). Each row records the results for a certain optimization method from different initialization strategies, with the best TrainErr or TestErr highlighted in bold. Each column provides the results of all the optimization methods with the same initial values, where the best TrainErr and TestErr are highlighted underline.

[Table 3a](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S5.T3.sf1 "In Table 3 ‣ 5.4 Comparisons with state-of-the-art GDs ‣ 5 Numerical experiments ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and Table 3c

demonstrate that for Synthetic dataset ($`T = 10`$) and Synthetic dataset ($`T = 500`$), no matter which initialization strategy is employed, our ALM method achieves the best TrainErr and TestErr among all the methods. [Table 3b](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S5.T3.sf2 "In Table 3 ‣ 5.4 Comparisons with state-of-the-art GDs ‣ 5 Numerical experiments ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") illustrates that our ALM achieves the best TrainErr under two types of initialization strategies, and obtains the best TestErr under three types of initialization strategies for Volatility of S$`\&`$P index. For any of the three datasets, our ALM achieves the best TrainErr and TestErr among all combinations of optimization methods and initialization strategies, which we highlight in blue.

We plot in [Fig. 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S5.F2 "In 5.4 Comparisons with state-of-the-art GDs ‣ 5 Numerical experiments ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") the TrainErr and TestErr versus CPU time measured in seconds using Volatility of S$`\&`$P index and Synthetic dataset ($`T = 500`$). Each line corresponds to a certain optimization method as indicated in the legend, with its most appropriate initialization strategy that leads to the final TestErr in bold as outlined in [Table 3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S5.T3 "In 5.4 Comparisons with state-of-the-art GDs ‣ 5 Numerical experiments ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). For the real world dataset, Volatility of S$`\&`$P index, the ALM achieves the smallest test error among all the methods. For the larger-scale Synthetic dataset ($`T = 500`$) with $`N_{\mathbf{w}} = {1.81 \times 10^{4}}`$, $`N_{\mathbf{a}} = {3.03 \times 10^{3}}`$ and $`r = 500`$, the ALM exhibits superior performance in terms of both training and test errors.

## 6 Conclusion

In this paper, the minimization model ([1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S1.E1 "Equation 1 ‣ 1 Introduction ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) for training RNNs is equivalently reformulated as problem ([3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E3 "Equation 3 ‣ 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) by using auxiliary variables. We propose the ALM in [Algorithm 1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg1 "In 3.1 ALM for the regularized RNNs ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") with [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") to solve the regularized problem ([7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "Equation 7 ‣ 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")). The BCD method in [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") is efficient for solving the subproblems of the ALM, which has a closed-form solution for each block problem. We establish the solid convergence results of the ALM to a KKT point of problem ([7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "Equation 7 ‣ 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")), as well as the finite termination of the BCD method for the subproblem of the ALM at each iteration. The efficiency and effectiveness of the ALM for training RNNs are demonstrated by numerical results with real world datasets and synthetic data, and comparison with state-of-art algorithms. An interesting further study is to extend our algorithm to a stochastic algorithm that is potential to deal with problems of huge samples efficiently. We believe that it is possible to extend our method and its corresponding analysis to other more complex RNN architectures, such as LSTMs, and will give rigorous analysis in the near future.

## Appendix A Proofs of the lemmas

### A.1 Proof of Lemma [2.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma 2.1. ‣ 2.2 Optimality conditions ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")

###### Proof A.1.

By direct computation,

```math
\begin{array}{r}
{{{{J\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s})}^{\top}\xi} + {\partial\left( {\zeta^{\top}\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s})}} \right)}} = \begin{bmatrix}
{J_{\mathbf{z}}\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s})}^{\top}\xi} \\
{{J_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s})}^{\top}\xi} + {J_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s})}^{\top}\zeta}} \\
{{J_{\mathbf{u}}\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s})}^{\top}\xi} + {\partial_{\mathbf{u}}\left( {\zeta^{\top}\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s})}} \right)}}
\end{bmatrix}},}
\end{array}
```

where

```math
{{{J_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s})}^{\top}\xi} + {J_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s})}^{\top}\zeta}} = \left\lbrack {{- {W^{\top}\xi_{2}}} + \zeta_{1}};\ldots;{{- {W^{\top}\xi_{T}}} + \zeta_{T - 1}};\zeta_{T} \right\rbrack},
```

```math
{{{J_{\mathbf{u}}\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s})}^{\top}\xi} + {\partial_{\mathbf{u}}\left( {\zeta^{\top}\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s})}} \right)}} = {\xi + {\partial_{\mathbf{u}}{({- {\zeta^{\top}{(\mathbf{u})}_{+}}})}}}}.
```

In order to achieve $`0 \in {{J\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s})}^{\top}\xi} + {\partial\left( {\zeta^{\top}\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s})}} \right)}}`$, it is necessary to require $`\zeta_{T} = 0`$, which is located in the last row of $`{J_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s})}^{\top}\xi} + {J_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s})}^{\top}\zeta}`$. Using $`\zeta_{T} = 0`$ and [Eq. 87](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#A1.E87 "In Proof A.1. ‣ A.1 Proof of Lemma 2.1 ‣ Appendix A Proofs of the lemmas ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), we find $`\xi_{T} = 0`$. Substituting the results into [Eq. 86](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#A1.E86 "In Proof A.1. ‣ A.1 Proof of Lemma 2.1 ‣ Appendix A Proofs of the lemmas ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and [Eq. 87](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#A1.E87 "In Proof A.1. ‣ A.1 Proof of Lemma 2.1 ‣ Appendix A Proofs of the lemmas ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") recursively and using [Eq. 86](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#A1.E86 "In Proof A.1. ‣ A.1 Proof of Lemma 2.1 ‣ Appendix A Proofs of the lemmas ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and [Eq. 87](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#A1.E87 "In Proof A.1. ‣ A.1 Proof of Lemma 2.1 ‣ Appendix A Proofs of the lemmas ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") equal $`0`$, we can derive that there exist no nonzero vectors $`\xi`$ and $`\zeta`$ such that $`0 \in {{J\mathcal{C}_{1}{(\mathbf{s})}^{\top}\xi} + {\partial\left( {\zeta^{\top}\mathcal{C}_{2}{(\mathbf{s})}} \right)}}`$.

### A.2 Proof of Lemma [2.5](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.Thmtheorem5 "Lemma 2.5. ‣ 2.3 Nonempty and compact solution set of Eq. 7 ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")

###### Proof A.2.

It is clear that $`0 \in {\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{R}}{(\rho)}}`$ and consequently $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{R}}{(\rho)}`$ is nonempty. Moreover,

$`{{{\| A\|}_{F}^{2} \leq {\rho/\lambda_{1}}},{{{\| W\|}_{F}^{2} \leq {\rho/\lambda_{2}}},{{\| V\|}_{F}^{2} \leq {\rho/\lambda_{3}}}}},`$$`{{{\| b\|}^{2} \leq {\rho/\lambda_{4}}},{{{\| c\|}^{2} \leq {\rho/\lambda_{5}}},{{\|\mathbf{u}\|}^{\mathbf{2}} \leq {\rho/\lambda_{6}}}}},`$

from $`{\mathcal{R}{(\mathbf{s})}} \leq \rho`$, $`{\ell{(\mathbf{s})}} \geq 0`$ and $`{P{(\mathbf{s})}} \geq 0`$. Hence for $`\mathbf{s} = {(\mathbf{z};\mathbf{h};\mathbf{u})} \in {\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{R}}{(\rho)}}`$, $`\mathbf{z}`$ and $`\mathbf{u}`$ are bounded, and consequently $`\mathbf{h}`$ is also bounded because $`\mathbf{h} = {(\mathbf{u})}_{+}`$.

Up to now, we have obtained the boundedness of $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{R}}{(\rho)}`$. By the continuity of $`\mathcal{R}{(\mathbf{s})}`$, we can assert that $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{R}}{(\rho)}`$ is closed according to \[[26](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib26), Theorem 1.6\]. Thus we can claim that the level set $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{R}}{(\rho)}`$ is nonempty and compact for any $`\rho > {\mathcal{R}{(0)}}`$. Then the solution set $`\mathcal{S}_{1}`$ is nonempty and compact according to \[[5](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib5), Proposition A.8\].

### A.3 Proof of Lemma [3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")

###### Proof A.3.

Statement [(i)](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.I1.i1 "Item (i) ‣ Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") can be easily obtained by the expression of $`\mathcal{L}{(\mathbf{s},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}`$ in ([3](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.Ex11 "3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) and the nonnegativity of $`\mathcal{R}{(\mathbf{s})}`$ in ([7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "Equation 7 ‣ 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")).

For statement [(ii)](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.I1.i2 "Item (ii) ‣ Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), the nonemptyness and closedness of the level set $`\Omega_{\mathcal{L}}{(\hat{\Gamma})}`$ are obvious. Moreover, we have $`\mathcal{R}{(\mathbf{s})}`$ and $`\|{{\mathbf{h} - {(\mathbf{u})}_{+}} + \frac{\zeta}{\gamma}}\|`$ are upper bounded for all $`\mathbf{s}`$ in $`\Omega_{\mathcal{L}}{(\hat{\Gamma})}`$. The function $`\mathcal{R}{(\mathbf{s})}`$ is upper bounded implies that $`\mathbf{w},\mathbf{a},\mathbf{u}`$ are bounded. Then the boundedness of $`\|{{\mathbf{h} - {(\mathbf{u})}_{+}} + \frac{\zeta}{\gamma}}\|`$ indicates that $`\mathbf{h}`$ is also bounded. Thus, $`\mathbf{s}`$ is bounded and statement [(ii)](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.I1.i2 "Item (ii) ‣ Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") holds.

Statements [(iii)](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.I1.i3 "Item (iii) ‣ Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and [(iv)](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.I1.i4 "Item (iv) ‣ Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") can be obtained by direct computation.

### A.4 Proof of Lemma [3.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.Thmtheorem2 "Lemma 3.2. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")

###### Proof A.4.

Using [Lemma 3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") [(iii)](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.I1.i3 "Item (iii) ‣ Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), we have

$`{{\nabla_{\mathbf{z}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{h}',\mathbf{u}',\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} - {{\nabla_{\mathbf{z}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{h},\mathbf{u},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}`$$`=`$$`\begin{bmatrix}
{{\gamma\Delta_{1}\mathbf{w}} - {\left( {{\Psi{(\mathbf{h}')}} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}}} \right)^{\top}\xi} - {\gamma\Delta_{3}}} \\
{{\frac{2}{T}{\sum_{t = 1}^{T}{\Delta_{2,t}\mathbf{a}}}} - {\frac{2}{T}{\sum_{t = 1}^{T}{\left( {{\Phi{(h_{t}')}} - {\Phi{(h_{t})}}} \right)^{\top}y_{t}}}}}
\end{bmatrix},`$

where $`\Delta_{1} = {{\Psi{(\mathbf{h}')}^{\top}\Psi{(\mathbf{h}')}} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}^{\top}\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}}}`$ and $`\Delta_{2,t} = {{\Phi{(h_{t}')}^{\top}\Phi{(h_{t}')}} - {\Phi{(h_{t})}^{\top}\Phi{(h_{t})}}}`$ and $`\Delta_{3} = {{\Psi{(\mathbf{h}')}\mathbf{u}'} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}\mathbf{u}}}`$. It is easy to see that

$`\|\Delta_{1}\|`$$`=`$$`\|{{{{\Psi{(\mathbf{h}')}^{\top}\Psi{(\mathbf{h}')}} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h}')}^{\top}\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}}} + {\Psi{(\mathbf{h}')}^{\top}\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}}} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}^{\top}\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}}}\|`$$`\leq`$$`{\left( {{\|{\Psi{(\mathbf{h}')}}\|} + {\|{\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}}\|}} \right){\|{{\Psi{(\mathbf{h}')}} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}}}\|}}.`$

Similarly, we have

$`\|\Delta_{2,t}\|`$$`\leq`$$`{{{\left( {{\|{\Phi{(h_{t}')}}\|} + {\|{\Phi{(h_{t})}}\|}} \right){\|{{\Phi{(h_{t}')}} - {\Phi{(h_{t})}}}\|}},{\forall t}} \in {\lbrack T\rbrack}},`$$`\|\Delta_{3}\|`$$`\leq`$$`{{{\|{\Psi{(\mathbf{h}')}}\|}{\|{\mathbf{u}' - \mathbf{u}}\|}} + {{\|\mathbf{u}\|}{\|{{\Psi{(\mathbf{h}')}} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}}}\|}}}.`$

Since $`{\mathbf{s},\mathbf{s}'} \in {\Omega_{\mathcal{L}}{(\hat{\Gamma})}}`$, we know that

$`{{{\ell{(\mathbf{s})}} + {P{(\mathbf{s})}} + {\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\| {{\mathbf{u} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}\mathbf{w}}} + \frac{\xi}{\gamma}} \right\|^{2}} + {\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\| {{\mathbf{h} - {(\mathbf{u})}_{+}} + \frac{\zeta}{\gamma}} \right\|^{2}}} \leq \delta}.`$

This, together with the expressions of $`\ell{(\mathbf{s})}`$ in ([7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E7 "Equation 7 ‣ 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) and $`P{(\mathbf{s})}`$ in ([6](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E6 "Equation 6 ‣ 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")), yields

$`{{\left\| W \right\|_{F} \leq \sqrt{\frac{\delta}{\lambda_{2}}}},{{\left\| \mathbf{a} \right\| \leq \sqrt{\frac{\delta}{\min\left\{ \lambda_{1},\lambda_{5} \right\}}}},{{\left\| \mathbf{w} \right\| \leq \sqrt{\frac{\delta}{\min\left\{ \lambda_{2},\lambda_{3},\lambda_{4} \right\}}}},{\left\| \mathbf{u} \right\| \leq \sqrt{\frac{\delta}{\lambda_{6}}}}}}}.`$

Moreover, since $`{{\|\mathbf{h}\|} - {\|{{(\mathbf{u})}_{+} - \frac{\zeta}{\gamma}}\|}} \leq {\|{{\mathbf{h} - {(\mathbf{u})}_{+}} + \frac{\zeta}{\gamma}}\|} \leq \sqrt{\frac{2\delta}{\gamma}}`$, we find

$`{{\|\mathbf{h}\|} \leq \delta_{0}}.`$

Using ([4](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S2.E4 "Equation 4 ‣ 2.1 Problem reformulation ‣ 2 Problem reformulation and optimality conditions ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")), we can easily obtain that

$`{{{\|{{\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}} - {\Psi{(\mathbf{h}')}}}\|} \leq {\sqrt{r}{\|{\mathbf{h}' - \mathbf{h}}\|}}},{{\|{{\Phi{(h_{t}')}} - {\Phi{(h_{t})}}}\|} \leq {\sqrt{m}{\|{h_{t}' - h_{t}}\|}}}},`$$`{{{\|{\Psi{(\mathbf{h})}}\|} = \sqrt{r{({{\|\mathbf{h}\|}^{2} + {\| X\|}^{2} + T})}}},{{\|{\Phi{(h_{t})}}\|} = \sqrt{m{({{\| h_{t}\|}^{2} + 1})}}}}.`$

Using the facts that for any $`{\iota_{1},\iota_{1},\ldots,\iota_{j}} \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, any $`{g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{j}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{n_{r}}`$, and any matrices $`{B_{1},B_{2},\ldots,B_{j}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{n_{c} \times n_{r}}`$, $`{\| B_{1}\|} \leq {\| B_{1}\|}_{F}`$, and

$`{{{\|{\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{(j)}{\iota_{j}B_{j}g_{j}}}\|} \leq {\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{j}{{|\iota_{j}|}{\| B_{j}\|}{\| g_{j}\|}}}},{{\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{j}{\|{\iota_{i}g_{i}}\|}} \leq {{\max\limits_{1 \leq i \leq j}{\{{|\iota_{i}|}\}}}\sqrt{j}{\|{(g_{1};\ldots;g_{j})}\|}}}},`$

taking the norm of both sides of ([89](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#A1.E89 "Equation 89 ‣ Proof A.4. ‣ A.4 Proof of Lemma 3.2 ‣ Appendix A Proofs of the lemmas ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")), and employing ([93](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#A1.E93 "Equation 93 ‣ Proof A.4. ‣ A.4 Proof of Lemma 3.2 ‣ Appendix A Proofs of the lemmas ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."))-([99](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#A1.E99 "Equation 99 ‣ Proof A.4. ‣ A.4 Proof of Lemma 3.2 ‣ Appendix A Proofs of the lemmas ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")), we can get ([15](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E15 "Equation 15 ‣ Lemma 3.2. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) with the expression of $`L_{1}{(\xi,\zeta,\gamma,\hat{r})}`$ in ([17](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E17 "Equation 17 ‣ Lemma 3.2. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) as desired.

Using [Lemma 3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") [(iv)](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.I1.i4 "Item (iv) ‣ Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), we have by direct computation

$`{{\nabla_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{h},\mathbf{u}',\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}} - {{\nabla_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{L}}{(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{h},\mathbf{u},\xi,\zeta,\gamma)}}`$$`=`$$`{{\gamma W^{T}{\sum\limits_{t = 1}^{T - 1}{({u_{t + 1} - u_{t + 1}'})}}} + {\gamma{\sum\limits_{t = 1}^{T}{({{(u_{t})}_{+} - {(u_{t}')}_{+}})}}}}.`$

Taking the norm of both sides of the above system of equations, employing ([96](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#A1.E96 "Equation 96 ‣ Proof A.4. ‣ A.4 Proof of Lemma 3.2 ‣ Appendix A Proofs of the lemmas ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")), ([100](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#A1.E100 "Equation 100 ‣ Proof A.4. ‣ A.4 Proof of Lemma 3.2 ‣ Appendix A Proofs of the lemmas ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")), and the facts $`{\|{{(u_{t})}_{+} - {(u_{t}')}_{+}}\|} \leq {\|{u_{t}' - u_{t}}\|}`$ for each $`t`$, we can get ([16](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E16 "Equation 16 ‣ Lemma 3.2. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) with $`L_{2}{(\xi,\zeta,\gamma,\hat{r})}`$ in the form of ([17](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E17 "Equation 17 ‣ Lemma 3.2. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")) as desired.

### A.5 Proof of Lemma [4.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma 4.1. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")

###### Proof A.5.

By [Eq. 27](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E27 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), [Eq. 28](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E28 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and [Eq. 29](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.E29 "In 4 ‣ Algorithm 2 ‣ 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), we know that for any $`j \in {\mathbb{N}}`$:

$`{{\mathcal{L}\left( \mathbf{s}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma \right)} \leq {\mathcal{L}\left( \mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{h}}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma \right)} \leq {\mathcal{L}\left( \mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma \right)} \leq {\mathcal{L}\left( \mathbf{s}^{({j - 1})},\xi,\zeta,\gamma \right)}}.`$

By the definition of $`\Gamma`$ in [Algorithm 2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#alg2 "In 3.2 BCD method for subproblem ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") and [Eq. 101](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#A1.E101 "In Proof A.5. ‣ A.5 Proof of Lemma 4.1 ‣ Appendix A Proofs of the lemmas ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), we can deduce that

$`{{{\mathcal{L}\left( \mathbf{s}^{(j)},\xi,\zeta,\gamma \right)} \leq \Gamma},{{\forall j} \in {\mathbb{N}}}}.`$

By the definition of $`\Omega_{\mathcal{L}}{(\Gamma)}`$ and [Lemma 3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") [(ii)](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.I1.i2 "Item (ii) ‣ Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), the proof is completed.

### A.6 Proof of Lemma [4.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Thmtheorem2 "Lemma 4.2. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")

###### Proof A.6.

It is clear that $`\Omega_{\mathcal{L}}{(\Gamma)}`$ is compact by [Lemma 3.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") [(ii)](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S3.I1.i2 "Item (ii) ‣ Lemma 3.1. ‣ 3 ALM with BCD method for Eq. 7 ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."). For the smooth part $`g`$ in $`\mathcal{L}`$, its gradient for those $`\mathbf{s} \in {\Omega_{\mathcal{L}}{(\Gamma)}}`$ is upper bounded. Now, let us turn to consider the nonsmooth part $`q`$ in $`\mathcal{L}`$. Let $`\mathbf{s} = {(\mathbf{z};\mathbf{h};\mathbf{u})}`$ and $`\mathbf{s}' = {(\mathbf{z}';\mathbf{h}';\mathbf{u}')}`$ be any two points in $`\Omega_{\mathcal{L}}{(\Gamma)}`$. We have

$`\left| {{q{(\mathbf{s}',\zeta,\gamma)}} - {q{(\mathbf{s},\zeta,\gamma)}}} \right|`$$`\leq`$$`\frac{\gamma}{2}\left| {\left\| {{\mathbf{h}' - {(\mathbf{u}')}_{+}} + \frac{\zeta}{\gamma}} \right\|^{2} - \left\| {{\mathbf{h} - {(\mathbf{u})}_{+}} + \frac{\zeta}{\gamma}} \right\|^{2}} \right|`$$`\leq`$$`\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\| {\mathbf{h}' - {(\mathbf{u}')}_{+} - {({\mathbf{h} - {(\mathbf{u})}_{+}})}} \right\|\left\| {{{{\mathbf{h}' - {(\mathbf{u}')}_{+}} + \mathbf{h}} - {(\mathbf{u})}_{+}} + {2\frac{\zeta}{\gamma}}} \right\|`$$`\leq`$$`{\left( {{2\gamma{\max\limits_{\mathbf{s} \in {\Omega_{\mathcal{L}}{(\Gamma)}}}{\{{{\|\mathbf{h}\|}_{\infty} + {\|\mathbf{u}\|}_{\infty}}\}}}} + {\|\zeta\|}} \right){({{\|{\mathbf{h}' - \mathbf{h}}\|} + {\|{\mathbf{u}' - \mathbf{u}}\|}})}}.`$

Up to now, we have proved the Lipschitz continuity of $`g`$ and $`q`$ on $`\Omega_{\mathcal{L}}{(\Gamma)}`$, which implies that $`\mathcal{L}`$ is Lipschitz continuous on $`\Omega_{\mathcal{L}}{(\Gamma)}`$.

The above result, together with the piecewise smoothness of function $`\mathcal{L}`$, yields that $`\mathcal{L}`$ is directionally differentiable on $`\Omega_{\mathcal{L}}{(\Gamma)}`$ by \[[21](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#bib.bib21)\].

### A.7 Proof of Lemma [4.7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Thmtheorem7 "Lemma 4.7. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).")

###### Proof A.7.

By [Eq. 42](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.E42 "In 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), the directional derivative of $`\mathcal{L}`$ at $`\overline{\mathbf{s}}`$ along $`d \in {\mathbb{R}}^{N_{\mathbf{w}} + N_{\mathbf{a}} + {2rT}}`$ refers to $`{\mathcal{L}'{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma;d)}} = {{g'{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\gamma;d)}} + {q'{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\zeta,\gamma;d)}}}`$. It is clear that

$`{{g'{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\gamma;d)}} = {{\langle{{\nabla_{\mathbf{z}}g}{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\gamma)}},d_{\mathbf{z}}\rangle} + {\langle{{\nabla_{\mathbf{h}}g}{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\gamma)}},d_{\mathbf{h}}\rangle} + {\langle{{\nabla_{\mathbf{u}}g}{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\gamma)}},d_{\mathbf{u}}\rangle}}}.`$

It remains to consider the directional derivative of nonsmooth part $`q`$. The function $`q`$ can be separated into $`rT`$ one dimensional functions with the same structure, i.e.,

```math
{{\phi{(\overline{h},\overline{u})}} = {{({{\overline{h} - {(\overline{u})}_{+}} + \nu_{1}})}^{2} - \nu_{1}^{2}}},
```

where $`{\overline{h},\overline{u}} \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ are variables and $`\nu_{1} \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ is a constant. The directional derivative of $`\phi`$ along the direction $`{({\overline{d}}_{1};{\overline{d}}_{2})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{2}`$ can be represented as the sum of the directional derivatives of $`\phi`$ along $`({\overline{d}}_{1};0)`$ and $`(0;{\overline{d}}_{2})`$ by the definition of directional derivative, i.e.,

$`\phi'\left( \overline{h},\overline{u};\left( {\overline{d}}_{1},{\overline{d}}_{2} \right) \right)`$$`= {\lim\limits_{\lambda \downarrow 0}\frac{\left( {{{\overline{h} + {\lambda{\overline{d}}_{1}}} - \left( {\overline{u} + {\lambda{\overline{d}}_{2}}} \right)_{+}} + \nu_{1}} \right)^{2} - \left( {{\overline{h} - \left( \overline{u} \right)_{+}} + \nu_{1}} \right)^{2}}{\lambda}}`$$`= {{{\phi'\left( \overline{h},\overline{u};\left( {\overline{d}}_{1},0 \right) \right)} + {\phi'\left( \overline{h},\overline{u};\left( 0,{\overline{d}}_{2} \right) \right)}} - {\lim\limits_{\lambda \downarrow 0}\frac{2\lambda{\overline{d}}_{1}\left( {\left( {u + {\lambda{\overline{d}}_{2}}} \right)_{+} - (u)_{+}} \right)}{\lambda}}}`$

where

$`\phi'\left( \overline{h},\overline{u};{({\overline{d}}_{1},0)} \right)`$$`= {\lim\limits_{\lambda \downarrow 0}\frac{\left( {{{\overline{h} + {\lambda{\overline{d}}_{1}}} - {(\overline{u})}_{+}} + \nu_{1}} \right)^{2} - \left( {{\overline{h} - {(\overline{u})}_{+}} + \nu_{1}} \right)^{2}}{\lambda}}`$$`{= {\lim\limits_{\lambda \downarrow 0}\frac{{({\overline{h} + {\lambda{\overline{d}}_{1}} + \nu_{1}})}^{2} - {({\overline{h} + \nu_{1}})}^{2} - {2{({\lambda{\overline{d}}_{1}})}{(\overline{u})}_{+}}}{\lambda}}},`$ $`\phi'\left( \overline{h},\overline{u};{(0,d_{2})} \right)`$$`= {\lim\limits_{\lambda \downarrow 0}\frac{\left( {{\overline{h} + \nu_{1}} - {({\overline{u} + {\lambda{\overline{d}}_{2}}})}_{+}} \right)^{2} - \left( {{\overline{h} + \nu_{1}} - {(\overline{u})}_{+}} \right)^{2}}{\lambda}}`$$`{= {\lim\limits_{\lambda \downarrow 0}\frac{{({\overline{u} + {\lambda{\overline{d}}_{2}}})}_{+}^{2} - {(\overline{u})}_{+}^{2} - {2{({\overline{h} + \nu_{1}})}\left( {{({\overline{u} + {\lambda{\overline{d}}_{2}}})}_{+} - {(\overline{u})}_{+}} \right)}}{\lambda}}},`$

and $`{\lim_{\lambda \downarrow 0}\frac{2\lambda{\overline{d}}_{1}{({{({u + {\lambda{\overline{d}}_{2}}})}_{+} - {(u)}_{+}})}}{\lambda}} = 0`$. By setting $`\overline{h} = {\overline{\mathbf{h}}}_{i}`$, $`\overline{u} = {\overline{\mathbf{u}}}_{i}`$, $`{\overline{d}}_{1} = {(d_{\mathbf{h}})}_{i}`$, $`{\overline{d}}_{2} = {(d_{\mathbf{u}})}_{i}`$, $`\nu_{1} = \frac{\zeta_{i}}{\gamma}`$, we have

$`q'{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\zeta,\gamma;\overline{d})}`$$`=`$$`\frac{\gamma}{2}{\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{rT}{\phi'\left( {\overline{\mathbf{h}}}_{i},{\overline{\mathbf{u}}}_{i};{({(d_{\mathbf{h}})}_{i},{(d_{\mathbf{u}})}_{i})} \right)}}`$$`=`$$`{\frac{\gamma}{2}{\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{rT}{\phi'\left( {\overline{\mathbf{h}}}_{i},{\overline{\mathbf{u}}}_{i};{({(d_{\mathbf{h}})}_{i},0)} \right)}}} + {\phi_{i}'\left( {\overline{\mathbf{h}}}_{i},{\overline{\mathbf{u}}}_{i};{(0,{(d_{\mathbf{u}})}_{i})} \right)}`$$`=`$$`{{q'\left( \overline{\mathbf{s}},\zeta,\gamma;{(0,d_{\mathbf{h}},0)} \right)} + {q'\left( \overline{\mathbf{s}},\zeta,\gamma;{(0,0,d_{\mathbf{u}})} \right)}}.`$

This, along with [Eq. 103](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#A1.E103 "In Proof A.7. ‣ A.7 Proof of Lemma 4.7 ‣ Appendix A Proofs of the lemmas ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027)."), yields that

$`\mathcal{L}'{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma;d)}`$$`=`$$`{\langle{{\nabla_{\mathbf{z}}g}{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\gamma)}},d_{\mathbf{z}}\rangle} + {\langle{{\nabla_{\mathbf{h}}g}{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\gamma)}},d_{\mathbf{h}}\rangle} + {\langle{{\nabla_{\mathbf{u}}g}{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\gamma)}},d_{\mathbf{u}}\rangle}`$$`{+ {q'\left( \overline{\mathbf{s}},\zeta,\gamma;{(0,d_{\mathbf{h}},0)} \right)}} + {q'\left( \overline{\mathbf{s}},\zeta,\gamma;{(0,0,d_{\mathbf{u}})} \right)}`$$`=`$$`{{\mathcal{L}'{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma;{(d_{\mathbf{z}},0,0)})}} + {\mathcal{L}'{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma;{(0,d_{\mathbf{h}},0)})}} + {\mathcal{L}'{(\overline{\mathbf{s}},\xi,\zeta,\gamma;{(0,0,d_{\mathbf{u}})})}}}.`$

Hence [Lemma 4.7](https://arxiv.org/html/2402.13687v2#S4.Thmtheorem7 "Lemma 4.7. ‣ 4.1 Convergence analysis of Algorithm 2 ‣ 4 Convergence analysis ‣ An Augmented Lagrangian Method for Training Recurrent Neural NetworksSubmitted on 28 December 2023. \\fundingThis work was supported in part by Hong Kong Research Grants Council C5036-21E and National Science Foundation of China (No. 12171027).") holds.
